built — completeness rules, one active release, and the catalog cache that reads the active release from the database (sprint 03, task S3-17, C106). v20 (2026-11-06) records the import of a catalog release as built — the command mode of the API host, what the import reuses, the set-once successor key and the guard against changes of stored rows (sprint 03, task S3-16, C105). v19 (2026-11-06) records the filter dimensions and rules of the Catalog module as built, with the seeded filter values and their keys, and removes the undefined field `AppType.IsInScope` (sprint 03, task S3-13, C104). migration step 2 after owner decision O-S3-5 on the design note of task S3-11: the existing criteria are not copied into the catalog (C102); it also names the two seed exclusions that step 2 drops by their entries (C103). v17 (2026-11-05) records **ADR-015**, the replacement of AutoMapper by hand-written maps (sprint 03, task S3-07, issues #31, #90, owner decision O-S3-4), and that the generic CRUD controllers are read-only since then (D11, C100). v16 (2026-11-05) carries the sprint-02 findings into this document (sprint 03, task S3-05, issue #180, `TECH_DEBT_v2.md` TD-14): the interim rule for O-22, Platform as built with Problem Details, `/api/Health` removed, the services of the hosting platform in the licence rule D12 (owner, sprint 03, task S3-03) and two open footer targets (O-23, O-24); no other rule changes. v15 (2026-10-30) records how sprint 02 built the module projects (tasks S2-06, S2-07): the module reference rules decided in S2-06, the Platform module as built and **ADR-014**, one `DbContext` and one migration history per module (owner decision O-S2-1, option A of S2-07); the owner accepted all rules of ADR-014 on the same day, with the review of PR #148. v14 (2026-10-29) records how sprint 01 built logging and the usage-event table ahead of step 1 (tasks S1-22, S1-23; `TECH_DEBT_v1.md` TD-11, issue #123). v13 (2026-10-27) records the owner's licence rule for dependencies (C89, D12): shipped dependencies must not use a strong copyleft or reciprocal licence; it follows the AutoMapper finding of sprint 01, task S1-03 (`docs/sprints/sprint_01/S1-03_backend_inventory.md` §4). v12 records the owner's confirmation of the SPEC proposals on 2026-10-19 (`SPEC_v2.md` SD19–SD23): catalog releases from data files without an editor UI, minimal administration of product identities (the owner holds `PlatformAdmin`), removal of candidates, supported browsers and all 🔧 default values; O-17–O-20 are closed. v11 incorporated the follow-ups of `SPEC_v1.md` §14 (2026-10-15): e-mail delivery through Lettermint (SMTP), e-mail confirmation and sign-in lockout, the dark theme in v1, the final shapes of the filter-options and criteria responses (O-8 closed), problem types, the personal data export, organisation and account rules, catalog import from data files, administration of product identities, removal of candidates and the final list of usage events (O-15 closed). v10 incorporated the review of the main documents before `SPEC_v1.md` (`pruefung_hauptdokumente_v1.md`, 2026-10-13): the owner's decisions D1–D7 (project copy of the criteria list, vendor entries without assessments, republication only by the original profile, content purge on consent withdrawal, context of experience reports, re-pinning, share links) and all findings of the consistency audit (A) and of the architecture and testing review (B), among them the session implementation, module projects, idempotency and the version reference in moderation. v9 recorded the session timeouts (O-12: 30 minutes idle, 8 hours absolute). v8 recorded the owner's decision on the session model (O-11: ASP.NET Core Identity cookie authentication with antiforgery) and clarifies what the locked criteria list allows. v7 incorporated the architecture review of 2026-10-12 (`architektur_review_v6.md`) and the owner's decisions on it: .NET 10, stable entries with immutable versions, stored assessment scope, module contracts, release-pinned procedure mapping, several test evidences per product, locked criteria lists, consent for every experience report. v6 incorporated the consistency audit before `SPEC_v1.md` (2026-10-12): the "tested according to" criterion type, the allowed-ratings union, app types and use cases on published entries (variant A), experience reports without counts, API contracts for multi-selection, and Angular 22. v5 added the catalog changes from the design review of 2026-10-06 (filter layers, exclusion rules, test types, BIT inklusiv rating scale, criterion texts per test procedure) and decision E13 (experience reports carry no test status). v4 recorded the owner's decisions on all three open questions of §16.1 and §16.3 and three further decisions that surfaced while writing the UX copy (step 7b).
> **Input documents:**
> - `prd_procureaccess_werteit_v5.md` (authoritative product scope)
> - `1_2_brainstorm_validierung_procureaccess_werteit_v2.md` (decision history)
> - `1_1_anforderungen_beschaffungstool_procureaccess_werteit_v2.md` (original requirements)
> - `CLAUDE.md` (state of the existing codebase)
> - `ux_copy_procureaccess_v1.md` (step 7b — screen inventory, domain components, UI terminology); current version `ux_copy_procureaccess_v41.md`
> - `design_handoff_procureaccess_v1.md` (v1.30) and `figma_code_mapping_procureaccess_v1.md` (v1.25, step 7f/7h replacement)
> - `kriterienkatalog_bit_inklusiv_v1.md` (1.5, + parts A and B) — criteria catalog based on BIT inklusiv, filter layers, exclusion rules, rating scale (German)
>
> **Diagrams:** FigJam board *ProcureAccess* — 01–09 architecture (component overview and module dependencies, Flows A–D, domain model per schema, migration path), 10–15 decision trees for §15 and §16, 16 persona flow map (PRD §8 user stories → flows and endpoints), 17 Flow E (direct Kanal-1 publication, new in v4). All boards were updated to v4 on 2026-10-02. On 2026-10-12, v7 versions of boards 01–09 and 17 were placed to the right of the originals (titles "NN (v7) …"); the originals stay as history and are marked "superseded". They cover four filter layers, procedure criteria and mapping, entry/version split, full assessment scope, test evidences, context, consent, module contracts, .NET 10 and Angular 22. Boards 10–16 (decision trees, persona flow map) remain at v4; their decisions are recorded in §16.
>
> **Language note:** Written in English per project convention. Domain terms are kept in German where they are the established project vocabulary (Kriterium, Workspace, Kanal 1/2, Vergabeunterlagen), with English identifiers at code level. User-facing terms are defined in the UX copy §2 (current version in the input list above) and differ deliberately from these (e.g. "Kanal 1" is never shown in the UI).
>
> - `architektur_review_v6.md` (architecture review of v6, German; findings R1–R6, Y1–Y8, G1–G4)
> - `pruefung_hauptdokumente_v1.md` (review of the main documents before `SPEC_v1.md`, German; owner decisions D1–D7, findings A-F1–A-F25 and B-F1–B-F19)
> - `SPEC_v2.md` (feature specification; v1 of 2026-10-15 with decisions SD1–SD18 and follow-ups §14; v2 of 2026-10-19 with decisions SD19–SD23 confirming the proposals O-S1–O-S4 and O-S6; change rows from v2.1 in the same file, current v2.25 of 2026-11-15)
>
> **Not covered here:** detailed UI/UX design (step 7), test strategy (step 6, `TESTING_v109.md`), feature-level acceptance criteria (step 8, `SPEC_v2.md`). Decisions marked **→ ADR** are candidates for formal decision records.

|---|---|---|
| C193 | **Removing a candidate and the Reader view as built (§5.4, §7.1, §7.2, §8.1, §8.4, §12 step 5; C76, C87, C179, C182, C184, C185, C188, C189; SPEC R-CAN-09, R-CAN-10 v2.67; UX copy v48 U190, U191; TESTING V397 – V403; WSP-04, TEN-05).** (1) *Endpoint (Workspace, no migration):* **`DELETE /candidates/{id}`** in `CandidateEndpoints` — Editor or Owner through `WorkspaceAccess` with the organisation of the stored candidate, `ConfirmedEmail`, antiforgery; `If-Match` with the candidate's tag as every change of a shared resource (C185; D3 (a)): 428 without it after the role check, 409 `conflict` for a stale one; 409 `project-archived`; 204. `CandidateService.RemoveAsync` runs in `ConcurrentChange.RunAsync`, removes the aggregate and stamps the project (`MarkChanged`; the project keeps its version, as for every change below it). (2) *What goes with it:* the foreign keys of C179 and C182 cascade in the database — the private section, the evidences with their private notes, the assessment rows; nothing is read first. The lock of C47 reads the assessment rows, so it is released when the last assessed candidate goes. (3) *Deferred to step 6 (C76, C87):* the published entry that stays visible with `SourceCandidateProductId` null; until a candidate can be published there is nothing to keep, and how the removal clears the link across the two modules is settled with the publication. (4) *Client, the removal:* `CandidatesApiService.remove(id, etag)` marks only `not-found` as the caller's; `CandidateOverview` shows `candidate.remove` as the last element below the cards (D4 (a)) for Editor and Owner of a project that is not archived, asks with the shared `ConfirmDialog`, then navigates to S32 — the route change moves the focus to the H1 — and announces `candidate.removed`; a 404 counts as removed; after any other failure (a stale tag shows the general error text, D3 (a)) it reads the candidate again into `CandidateLoad`, so the tab shows what is stored and the next try carries its tag. No state in `withWorkspace()`: S32 reads the project on entry. (5) *Client, the Reader view (R-CAN-10):* the two tabs decide `editable` (Editor or Owner, project not archived) apart from what they show. `CandidateEvidence` has a second branch without the form: the question with the stored answer and one card per evidence as `<dl>`s, the optional facts under the display headings of U190 and only when filled, the private note with the text of the private section. `CandidateAssessment` reads the assessments for every member and, when not `editable`, leaves out the intro, the filter, the union note and the link „Prüfnachweis bearbeiten“ and shows each criterion as a card with its title (H2), „Einschätzung“ and, when there is one, „Begründung“ (U191) instead of K6; nothing is saved or read again when the tab is left. Both views also serve an archived project (R-PRJ-05), which showed an empty tab before (C188 (7), C189 (7)). (6) *Not built:* the publish button and `candidate.remove.publishedNote` (step 6); S37 on the removal (D3 (a)) | R-CAN-09, R-CAN-10; SD21; owner decisions S6-22 D2 (a) – D4 (a) |

(2) *K6* `pa-assessment-input` (`shared/components/assessment-input/`, Handoff §4.6): a fieldset with the criterion title as legend; `layout` `radios` (from 600 px: the question `candidate.assessment.legend` linked to the radio group, the note field always shown) or `compact` (below: a select „Einschätzung“, the note behind „Begründung hinzufügen/bearbeiten“), set by the page through `injectWideLayout()`; only the server's `allowedRatings` and „Nicht eingeschätzt“ last (C31); `value` and `note` are models. (3) *State that survives navigation:* `withWorkspace()` gains `assessments { candidateId, status, view }` (C184 (2) foresaw it): `loadAssessments` reads `GET /candidates/{id}/assessments` and shows the last state of the same candidate at once while it reads again; `changeAssessment` applies a change at once and saves the whole row 800 ms after its last change; the saves of one row run one after another (the server's last write wins, R-CAN-11, so the client's last change must be the last request); a read keeps the rows still waiting or on their way; a row whose note is too long is kept but not sent; `flushAssessments` sends every waiting row when the tab is left; `resetWorkspace` drops waiting rows. (4) *Failures:* a failed read shows the S80 text as an error notice in the tab; a failed save keeps the error interceptor's system states (the general text, `project-archived`, `email-unconfirmed`) and the change on screen — the UX copy has no text of its own for a failed autosave. (5) *Überblick:* after leaving the tab the candidate is read again and only `progress` and `fulfillment` are taken into the route's `CandidateLoad` — its tag and fields stay as the other tabs read them (SPEC v2.59-3), and nothing is counted on the client. (6) *Derived rows* (C46, CAT-17): one card per `procedureAnswers` item after the K6 cards, the title in K6's title style, the value `assessment.procedure.derived.yes|no|notStated` on its own line, the hint and a link to `pruefnachweis` described by the title; no input. (7) *Roles:* only Editor and Owner of a project that is not archived get the content (Q3 (a); Readers with S6-22). (8) *Left for S6-19b:* progress with the list line (Q2 (a)), the filter „Nur noch nicht eingeschätzte“, `pa-rating-legend` expanded until the first assessment with its state as a stored user setting (Q1 (b): a fifth member of `UserPreferences` in IdentityAccess, SPEC §8.12) | S6-19a; owner decisions S6-19 Q1 (b) – Q3 (a) |

(2) *One „Speichern“ (D1 (a))* sequences the endpoints of S6-15 with the tags of C185 instead of a new batch endpoint: first `PUT …/test-status` when the status changed, with the candidate's tag as the tab read it — leaving „Ja“ sends `removeEvidences: true` when the user removed saved evidences, because separate `DELETE`s would change the candidate's tag first; a 409 `test-status-conflict` with `evidenceCount` asks `candidate.evidence.list.statusChange` with that count and repeats with `removeEvidences: true` —, then `DELETE` of the evidences the user removed while „Ja“ stays, each with its own tag (a 404 counts as removed), then `POST` for each new card (no tag) and `PUT` for each changed card (its tag), in card order. An ETag `conflict` (409) stops the sequence with the general text until S37 (S6-21). The sequence is not atomic, on purpose: what the server took stays saved, a refused card keeps its input and its errors, and the candidate is read again afterwards and handed to `CandidateLoad`, so Überblick shows the stored state and the next „Speichern“ has the current tag. A batch endpoint would make it atomic but needs a server change, a new contract and its ETag rules (S6-20); for a handful of cards per product the sequence suffices. (3) *Cards:* `EvidenceCard` (`evidence-card.ts`) holds the form controls and the last stored evidence; a new card nobody typed into is skipped (D4 (b)), so „Ja“ without evidence stays possible (WSP-01: 0..n). Leaving „Ja“ asks before the cards go (D2 (a)); the removal reaches the server with „Speichern“. „One per procedure“ is checked on the client before sending; a 400 at `testTypeId` is shown as `candidate.evidence.list.duplicate`. (4) *Procedures:* `TEST_PROCEDURES` in `models/candidates.ts` is a client copy of the five keys the catalog seeds as filter values (`FilterValueSeed.TestTypes`, C104), because `GET /catalog/filter-options` answers 404 while no release is active (§16.5 follow-up: serve the procedures without a release). (5) *Dates:* a text field `TT.MM.JJJJ` with `shared/date/german-date.ts` (parse, format, today in the browser; the server checks Europe/Berlin) instead of Material's datepicker, whose calendar texts the UX copy does not have yet (D6 (a)). (6) *Error summary:* `ErrorSummaryItem.field.group` names the part of a form a repeated label belongs to („Prüfnachweis 2 – Wer hat geprüft?: …“). (7) *Roles:* Editor and Owner of a project that is not archived get the form; Readers and archived projects see the empty tab until S6-22 builds the read-only view (D5 (a)). (8) `ProblemDetails.evidenceCount` (SPEC §8.6 v2.53) is typed on the client; `CandidatesApiService` sends `If-Match` for the status and for changing and removing an evidence (open point 3 of S6-20a). | R-CAN-05 needs the evidences on the client; the frames have one save for the tab (D1 (a)); the server endpoints of S6-15 stay as they are |

### Changes from v98

Sprint 06, task S6-20b (`docs/sprints/sprint_06/S6-20b_etag_saved_lists.md`; owner decisions S6-20 D1 (a) – D4 (a) of 2026-11-18, taken with S6-20a; no new decision).

| # | Change | Reason |
|---|---|---|
| C187 | **Optimistic locking of the saved lists and the project's copy as built (§5.3, §7.1, §7.2, §8.1, §12 step 5; C53, C155, C156, C180, C185; SPEC R-SAV-03, R-PRJ-07, R-CAN-11 v2.61; TESTING V369 – V373; API-03, API-04).** (1) *Version and editor:* `CriteriaConfiguration` has `Version` (EF concurrency token of the row) and `ModifiedByUserId` (migration `ListVersions` of the Configurator context; existing lists start at version 1 without an editor); a new list and a project's copy start at version 1 with their creator. A stored decision and a selection change count a version (`MarkEdited`); a decision counts also when it repeats the stored values, as it stamps the last change. (2) *Requests:* `GET /configurations/{id}` carries the `ETag` header of C185's format (`EntityTags`); the lists of `GET /configurations` (S14) name theirs as the body member `etag`. `PATCH /configurations/{id}/criteria/{criterionRevisionId}`, `PUT /configurations/{id}/selection` and `DELETE /configurations/{id}` require `If-Match` — also on a project's copy, which is changed through the same endpoints (C51). Order: role check (404, 403) → 428 `precondition-required` → the stored list: 409 `conflict` with `editorDisplayName` for a tag that is not the stored version's, before the criterion is looked for (a criterion a selection change removed meanwhile is a conflict, not a 404), before `project-copy` and before the validation. The `PATCH` keeps 204 and answers with the new `ETag`, the `PUT` 200 with it. Saving a new list, copying one to the active release and the share links need none. (3) *Concurrent saves — the race of S5-21a R1:* the comparison happens after the load; a save that then meets a change stored since (another decision, a selection change that removed the criterion, a deletion) fails with `DbUpdateConcurrencyException`, which `ConfigurationService.SaveAsync` answers as the conflict with who made the stored version, or as 404 when the list is gone — before, a decision racing a selection change answered 500. No second attempt as in the Workspace (C185 (4)): nothing stamps a list from below. (4) *Replacing a project's copy* (`ProjectCriteriaLists`, C180): the replacement does not depend on the copy's version — the project's `If-Match` guards the assignment (C185) —, so a copy that got a new version or went since it was loaded is replaced on a second attempt, as a lost insert race already was. (5) *Client:* `ConfiguratorApiService.get` keeps the header as `SavedList.etag`; `decide(…, etag)` resolves to the new tag, `changeSelection(…, etag)` and `delete(…, etag)` send theirs (S10 of a saved list from `SavedListSelection`, S14 from the list's `etag`). `SavedListEditor` saves one change at a time for the whole list — before, one chain per criterion —, each with the tag the save before answered; after a failed save the tag stays, so a conflict stays one until the list is loaded again (S37, S6-21). **API-03 is complete** with this change | S6-20b |

### Changes from v96

Sprint 06, task S6-20a (`docs/sprints/sprint_06/S6-20a_etag_concurrency.md`; owner decisions S6-20 D1 (a) – D4 (a) of 2026-11-18: D1 (a) — the ETag comes from a version column per resource, not from `xmin` · D2 (a) — which requests need `If-Match`, the two `DELETE`s and the list assignment included · D3 (a) — 428 as the new problem type `precondition-required` without a UX key · D4 (a) — S6-20 is split by module: S6-20a the shared parts and the Workspace module with its client calls, S6-20b the saved lists and the project copy in Configurator).

| # | Change | Reason |
|---|---|---|
| C185 | **Optimistic locking of the workspace resources as built (§5.4, §7.1, §7.2, §9.2, §12 step 5; C8, C78, C172, C179, C180, C182; SPEC R-PRJ-07, R-CAN-11, §8.6 v2.59; TESTING V357 – V362; API-03, API-04).** (1) *The tag (D1 (a); replaces „derived from `xmin`“ of §7.1):* `ProcurementProject`, `CandidateProduct` and `TestEvidence` have an integer `Version` — the EF concurrency token of the row — and `ModifiedByUserId` (migration `ResourceVersions` of the Workspace context; existing rows start at version 1 without an editor). The ETag is the strong tag `"<epoch>-<version>"` (`EntityTags` in `Platform.Contracts/Concurrency`, one instance registered by Platform from the 🔧 `Concurrency:ETagEpoch`, at least 1, checked at the start). `xmin` would change with every write of the row, also when a change below only stamps the last change on its parent (S6-15 5 (a), S6-18 3 (a)): an autosaved assessment would make the tags of its candidate and project stale although it overwrites nothing of them. (2) *What counts a version* (`MarkEdited(userId)`, called by the services): of a project its name, its status and the assignment of a list; of a candidate its facts, its private section, its test status and an evidence added or removed — the set of evidences belongs to the test status (WSP-01), so a status change never meets an evidence its sender has not seen; of an evidence its fields and its private note. A change with the same values counts nothing. Changes below (a candidate, an evidence, an assessment) only stamp `ModifiedAt` of the parents. (3) *Requests (D2 (a)):* every answer with a project or a candidate carries its `ETag` header (also 201); an evidence, read only inside its candidate, names its tag as the body member `etag`, and the answers of adding and changing it carry it as header too. `If-Match` is required by `PATCH /projects/{id}` and `PUT /projects/{id}/criteria-configuration` (the project's tag; the answer's ETag is the project's new one), `PATCH /candidates/{id}` and `PUT /candidates/{id}/test-status` (the candidate's), `PUT` and `DELETE /test-evidences/{id}` (the evidence's). Creating requests and `PUT …/assessments/{id}` need none. Order: role check (404, 403) → **428 `precondition-required`** without the header (D3 (a)) → validation → the stored row: **409 `conflict`** with `editorDisplayName` when the header is not exactly the tag of the stored version (`*`, a weak tag and a list match nothing), before the archived check. The name comes from `IUserDirectory` (`IdentityAccess.Contracts`) for the row's `ModifiedByUserId`; it is `null` for a row without an editor or a deleted account. (4) *Concurrent saves:* a save that meets a row changed since it was loaded fails with `DbUpdateConcurrencyException`; `ConcurrentChange.RunAsync` (Workspace) drops the tracked rows and runs the change again, at most three times: the fresh load refuses a stale `If-Match` with the conflict, and a save that only stamped a parent lands on the row as it is now. The assessment `PUT` keeps its one retry (C182) and stays last-write-wins. (5) *List assignment:* Configurator stores the copy before the project counts its version, so a refused source leaves the sender's tag valid; a project change that lands between the check and the version answers 409 although the copy is replaced (two contexts, no common transaction — as the lock of C182). (6) *Restore:* raising `Concurrency:ETagEpoch` makes every tag issued before stale (§9.2); a version counter is restored with its row, so the epoch is still what tells a tag of before the restore from one of after. (7) *Client:* `core/concurrency/etag.ts` (`withEtag`, `ifMatch`); `ProjectsApiService` and `CandidatesApiService` keep the header as `etag` on `Project` and `Candidate` and send it with `PATCH /candidates/{id}` and the list assignment (S31 assigns with the tag of the project it has just created). A 409 `conflict` shows the general error text until S37 (S6-21). The client image's nginx must keep `/api/` uncompressed: nginx turns a strong ETag into a weak one when it compresses. **Deferred to S6-20b:** `CriteriaConfiguration` (saved lists and the project's copy, Configurator context) and the S11 editor | S6-20a; owner decisions D1 (a) – D4 (a) |

### Changes from v93

Sprint 06, task S6-18 (`docs/sprints/sprint_06/S6-18_assessment_server.md`; owner decisions S6-18 1 (a) – 5 (a) of 2026-11-18: 1 (a) — the rows are read by an endpoint of their own, progress and counts ride on the project and candidate views · 2 (a) — only an included requirement criterion of the project's list can be assessed, one 400 for everything else · 3 (a) — an assessment moves the last change of candidate and project · 4 (a) — replacing the list deletes the rows without a value · 5 (a) — the server says whether the ratings come from several procedures). No change of the module table: Workspace already depends on `Catalog.Contracts` and `Configurator.Contracts` (§4.3).

| # | Change | Reason |
|---|---|---|
| C182 | **Assessments of candidate products on the server as built (§5.1, §5.4, §7.2, §7.3, §12 step 5; C30, C31, C34, C47, C51, C53, C67, C179, C180; SPEC R-CAN-06 – R-CAN-08, R-PRJ-04 v2.56; TESTING V342 – V347; CAT-15, CAT-17, SIG-13, WSP-02, WSP-03).** (1) *Entity (Workspace context, migration `CandidateAssessments`):* `CandidateAssessment` with the key (`CandidateProductId`, `CriterionRevisionId`) — one row per candidate and criterion revision of the release the project's copy is pinned to —, `OrganizationId` with the organisation filter, `Fulfillment` by name (`Unknown` and the six levels), `EvidenceNote?` ≤ 1 000, `ModifiedAt`; a foreign key with cascade to the candidate, none to the revision (the catalog is another context, ADR-014). *Lazy (C53):* a row is stored with the first value or note and deleted when it has neither; a row with `Unknown` (a note without a value) is no assessment. (2) *Nothing of the list is copied into the workspace:* `AssessmentReader` reads the project's copy (`IProjectCriteriaLists`, `IConfigurationReader`) and resolves its selection under its stored release (`ICatalogSelectionResolver`) on every request; the included requirement criteria are what is assessed and counted, the included procedure criteria what is derived. So an adjusted copy shows at once (C51): a newly included criterion is `Unknown` for every candidate, an excluded one leaves the views while its row stays and reappears unchanged (WSP-03). (3) *Catalog.Contracts:* `ResolvedCatalogCriterion` gains `AllowedRatings` — the union of C31 that `GET /catalog/criteria` already computes, none for a procedure criterion — and `TestTypeKey` of a procedure criterion; `ResolvedCatalogSelection.MappedTestTypeKeys` names the procedures the release maps to the selected app types. (4) *Endpoints (§7.2):* **`GET /candidates/{id}/assessments`** (every member) → `{ candidateId, releaseVersion, ratingsFromSeveralProcedures, progress { done, total }, criteria[] { criterionRevisionId, number, title, plainLanguageTitle, productPart, allowedRatings[], value, note }, procedureAnswers[] { criterionRevisionId, testTypeId, title, answer } }` in list order; `releaseVersion` is `null` and the lists are empty while the project has no list. **`PUT /candidates/{id}/assessments/{criterionRevisionId}`** `{ value, note? }` (Editor or Owner, `ConfirmedEmail`, antiforgery) → 200 `{ criterionRevisionId, value, note }`: the whole row, the last write wins (no `If-Match`, also not with S6-20; two first writes at once meet at the key and the loser is repeated). Errors: 400 `validation` at `value` (unknown name, or outside the union — CAT-15), at `note`, or at `criterionRevisionId` for anything but an included requirement criterion of the project's list — a procedure criterion (CAT-17), an excluded or unknown criterion, a project without a list (owner decision 2 (a)); 409 `project-archived`. (5) *Counts (C34, SIG-13):* `GET /projects/{id}` carries per candidate `progress`, `fulfillment` — exactly `fulfilled`, `slightRestriction`, `restriction`, `barrier`, `blockade`, `notApplicable`, `unknown`, which add up to the included requirement criteria — and `procedureAnswers[]` (C67: `yes` with an evidence of the procedure, `no` when not tested, else `notStated`); `GET /candidates/{id}` carries `progress` and `fulfillment`; all three are `null` while the project has no list. One query for all candidates of a project. (6) *The lock (C47; replaces the hook of C180 (4)):* `ProjectCriteriaListService` refuses to replace the copy once a candidate of the project has a row with a value other than `Unknown` (409 `locked-criteria-list`); `ICriteriaListLock` is gone. A successful replace deletes the project's rows without a value, which belong to criteria of the replaced copy (owner decision 4 (a)). (7) *Last change:* a changed assessment moves `ModifiedAt` of the candidate and of its project (owner decision 3 (a), as C179 (7)); S6-20 must therefore not derive the candidate's `ETag` from `ModifiedAt` alone, or an autosave makes an open candidate form stale. (8) *Known gap:* the copy is stored by Configurator's context, the assessments by Workspace's, so lock check and copy are not one transaction; an assessment saved in between keeps its value and locks the new list, in which its criterion may not exist. Closing it needs a row lock on the project (raw SQL, TEN-02 allow-list) or the `If-Match` S6-20 gives the project copy — open point 1 of S6-18. (9) *Left for later:* the assessment tab S34b (S6-19), the counts on S32 (S6-14), removing a candidate with its assessments (S6-22, the cascade is in place), published assessments (step 6) | S6-18; owner decisions S6-18 1 (a) – 5 (a) |

The mode hides „Auswahl ändern“, „Freigabelink erstellen“ and the older-release notice (the server refuses them with `project-copy`, C180), shows `criteria.list.projectCopy`, and is read-only for a Reader and for an archived project (D3 (a)). The saved-list resolver turns a copy's id into a redirect to its project's address (D7 (a)), so a copy has one place. (2) *Store:* `withWorkspace()` gains `assignCriteriaList` (the PUT of C180) and `criteriaAdded` — the number of criteria that were left out when S11 of a copy opened and are in the list when it is left, kept in memory for the next S32 of that project (`noteCriteriaAdded` / `takeCriteriaAdded`), so any way back reports it once (`projects.detail.criteriaAdded`, C51). (3) *S31* offers the organisation's saved lists (copies are never listed, C180) with „Keine Liste“ and assigns the chosen one right after creating the project; a failed copy opens S32 with the general notice (D5 (b)). |---|---|---|
| C180 | **Project copy of a criteria list on the server as built (§4.3, §5.3, §5.4, §7.2, §12 step 5, ADR-014; C47, C51, C53, C59, C172; SPEC R-PRJ-02, R-PRJ-04, R-PRJ-06, R-SAV-02, R-SAV-04, §8.6, §8.10 v2.54; TESTING V335 – V338).** (1) *Link on the copy only (D1 (a)):* `CriteriaConfiguration.ProcurementProjectId` (`uuid`, nullable) in `configurator."CriteriaConfigurations"` (migration `ProjectCopies`) with a unique index where set, so a project owns at most one copy. **No foreign key** — the project lives in Workspace's context and no key crosses two contexts (ADR-014); §5.3's „the column and its foreign key“ is corrected. **`ProcurementProject.CriteriaConfigurationId` (§5.4) is not built:** with a pointer on both sides, assigning would save in two contexts without one transaction, and the two could disagree; with the id on the copy, replacing deletes the old copy and stores the new one in one save of `ConfiguratorDbContext`. Workspace has no migration in this task. (2) *Contract (`Configurator.Contracts`):* `IProjectCriteriaLists` — `CopyIntoProjectAsync(source, project, organisation, user)` and `FindOfProjectAsync(project)` → `{ id, name, releaseVersion }` — implemented by Configurator, used by Workspace (Workspace → Configurator.Contracts is in §4.3; Configurator still depends on no Workspace project). (3) *Copy:* `CriteriaConfiguration.CopyForProject` copies organisation, name, release, the four selection layers and every row with its stored recommendation (CAT-08) and decision; a new id, the assigning user as creator, the time of the assignment. The source is not changed (D1). A copy as source throws. (4) *Endpoint (Workspace, `Projects/ProjectCriteriaListEndpoint`):* `PUT /projects/{id}/criteria-configuration` `{ sourceConfigurationId }` → 200 `{ id, name, releaseVersion }`. Editor on the stored project's organisation (404 / 403, TEN-05), confirmed address (SEC-10), antiforgery; then 400 `validation` at `sourceConfigurationId` (missing, unknown, of another organisation — also for a member of both — or a project copy: one answer for all), 409 `project-archived` (R-PRJ-05), 409 `locked-criteria-list` with a field error at `sourceConfigurationId` once the lock says so (C47). Two first assignments at once: the one that loses the unique index retries once and replaces the winner's copy (the last change wins until API-03, S6-20). `GET /projects/{id}` (and the PATCH answer) carry `criteriaList` — `{ id, name, releaseVersion }` or `null`. The project's `ModifiedAt` does not move with an assignment (C172 open point 3 stays open). (5) *Lock hook (C47):* `ICriteriaListLock` in Workspace, registered as `NoAssessmentsYet` („not locked“) until S6-18 replaces the registration by the query on its assessment rows; the 409 branch is tested with a test double. (6) *The copy at the saved-list endpoints (C51, D2 (a)):* read (`procurementProjectId` in the answer) and `PATCH …/criteria/{rev}` as for any list; `PUT …/selection`, `DELETE` and `POST …/copies` answer **409 `project-copy`** (new in `ProblemTypes`; the copy goes only when the project's list is replaced, and it is never a source); `POST …/share-links` answers 409 `project-copy` after the role check and stores nothing (C59, TEN-08); the saved export works; `GET /configurations?organizationId=` leaves copies out (R-SAV-02). (7) *Not built here:* S11 in project-copy mode, the list choice of S31 and `projects.detail.criteriaAdded` (S6-12b); the server's refusal of a change to the copy of an archived project (D3 (a): Configurator cannot see the project's status; open point); the copies in the personal data export are listed like other lists the user created, without their project (open point) | Owner decisions S6-12 D1 – D4; R-PRJ-02, R-PRJ-04, R-PRJ-06 |
### Changes from v90

Sprint 06, task S6-15 (`docs/sprints/sprint_06/S6-15_candidates_server.md`; owner decisions S6-15 1 (a) – 5 (a) of 2026-11-18; SPEC v2.53; TESTING V329 – V334).

| # | Change | Reason |
|---|---|---|
| C179 | **Candidate products on the server as built (§4.3, §5.4, §7.2, §12 step 5; C27, C46, C65, C76; SPEC R-CAN-02, R-CAN-05, §7.2, §8.6 v2.53; WSP-01, SIG-03).** (1) *Entities (Workspace context, migration `CandidateProducts`):* `CandidateProduct` (`Id`, `OrganizationId` of its project, `ProcurementProjectId` with a foreign key and cascade, `SoftwareProductId?` without a foreign key and always `null` until „Merken“ and the link come with step 6 — owner decision S6-15 1 (a), so that step needs no workspace migration for it —, `Name` ≤ 200, `VendorName?` ≤ 200, `WebsiteUrl?` `https` ≤ 2 048, `FunctionalSummary?` ≤ 2 000, `TestStatus` by name, `NOT NULL DEFAULT 'Unknown'`, `CreatedAt`, `ModifiedAt`); only the name is required (owner decision S6-15 2 (a)). `TestEvidence` (`TestKind` Procedure / Other / Own by name, `TestTypeKey?` — the key of a catalog procedure, which belongs to no release (C104) —, `OtherTestTypeName?` ≤ 120, `TestedByName` ≤ 200 and `TestDate` required, `TestedVersion?` ≤ 200, `EvidenceUrl?`, `CreatedAt`); no `AttachmentId` yet. A partial unique index (candidate, `TestTypeKey`) where the key is set: at most one evidence per catalog procedure, several „Andere Prüfung“ and „Eigene Prüfung“. Every table carries `OrganizationId` and the organisation filter, so a child read by its id needs no join (TEN-01). (2) *Private rows (deviates from the column list above):* contacts and internal notes are the row `CandidatePrivateSection` (key = candidate, cascade), the qualification note of an evidence the row `TestEvidencePrivateNote` (key = evidence, cascade; ≤ 2 000, owner decision S6-15 4 (a)). Neither `CandidateProduct` nor `TestEvidence` has a member that leads to them, so the copy of Flow B step 2 cannot carry a private field (PRV-01, PRV-02 structural; a unit test checks the two types). (3) *Aggregate (WSP-01):* the candidate holds its evidences and alone adds, changes and removes them: an evidence only while the status is `Tested`, no second of the same procedure, a new product version replaces the fields of the same row (C65); leaving `Tested` while evidences exist is refused unless the change asks to remove them. (4) *Endpoints (§7.2):* `POST /projects/{id}/candidates`, `GET` and `PATCH /candidates/{id}` (a change names what changes, an empty text clears an optional field), `PUT /candidates/{id}/test-status { testStatus, removeEvidences? }`, `POST /candidates/{id}/test-evidences`, `PUT` and `DELETE /test-evidences/{id}`; the project read lists its candidates (`candidates[]`: id, name, vendor, test status) and counts them (`productCount`). Role check first (Reader reads, Editor changes, the organisation taken from the stored row), then the validation; `ConfirmedEmail` and antiforgery on every change; an archived project's candidates answer 409 `project-archived`. (5) *Problem type `test-status-conflict`* (409, field error `testStatus`, extension `evidenceCount`; owner decision S6-15 3 (a)): an evidence while the status is not `Tested`, or leaving `Tested` with evidences without `removeEvidences`; the client asks `candidate.evidence.list.statusChange` and repeats with `removeEvidences: true`. A duplicate procedure is 400 `validation` at `testTypeId`. (6) *Date:* a test date may not lie after today in 🔧 `Workspace:TimeZone` (Europe/Berlin). (7) *Last change:* a change of a candidate, its private section, its test status or an evidence moves the `ModifiedAt` of the candidate and of its project (owner decision S6-15 5 (a)). (8) *Catalog.Contracts:* `ICatalogTestTypes` gives the keys of the test procedures from the cached filter values. (9) *Left for later:* „Merken“ and the name-match hint `candidate.form.name.match` with the directory (R-CAN-01, M6); removing a candidate (S6-22, C76); `ETag`/`If-Match` (S6-20); attachments; assessments (S6-18) | S6-15; owner decisions S6-15 1 (a) – 5 (a) |

### Changes from v88

Sprint 06, task S6-26 (`docs/sprints/sprint_06/S6-26_rate_limits.md`; issue #318, TECH_DEBT TD-60; owner decision O-S6-2 of 2026-11-18 and the task decisions Q1 (a) and Q2 (a) of the same day).

| # | Change | Reason |
|---|---|---|
| C177 | **Rate limits of sign-in and organisation creation (§9.2; C126, C139, C141, C147; SPEC §7.3, §8.8, §8.9 v2.51; TD-60, #318).** Three new policies of `RateLimitPolicies` (`Platform.Contracts`), each with its 🔧 `RateLimits:<policy>` in `appsettings.json`: `SignIn` per client IP (30 per 10 minutes) on `POST /api/v1/auth/login` with `RequireRateLimiting`; `SignInAddress` per e-mail address (10 per hour), a keyed policy that `AuthEndpoints.LoginAsync` asks through `IKeyedRateLimiter` with the trimmed, normalised address after the field check and **before** it looks the address up, so the 429 is the same for an address with and without an account and an attempt it refuses never reaches the password check (no lockout count); `OrganizationCreation` per signed-in user (5 per day, window `1.00:00:00`) on `POST /api/v1/organizations`, behind the authorization, so an unconfirmed account's 403 does not count. All three answer the usual 429 `rate-limit` with `retryAfterMinutes` and `Retry-After` (API-05); the client's sign-in form and S20 show `errors.rateLimit` (for the day window „1440 Minuten“, task decision Q2 (a)). The integration host and `docker-compose.e2e.yml` relax them like the other policies; `RateLimits:Invitation` is unchanged. A known consequence of the per-address limit: whoever knows an address can block its sign-in for up to an hour with ten requests (the lockout already allowed 15 minutes with five); accepted with O-S6-2. | Sign-in had only the per-account lockout, which neither limits guessing across many addresses nor the load on the password hasher (S5-07 P4); one confirmed account could create any number of organisations (S5-16a P3). The values are the owner's (O-S6-2): not like the reset's 5 per hour per IP, which would lock out an office behind one shared address. |

### Changes from v87

Sprint 06, task S6-05 (`docs/sprints/sprint_06/S6-05_mail_token_hygiene.md`; issues #382, #383, #381, TECH_DEBT TD-58, TD-59, TD-57; owner decision O-S6-3 of 2026-11-18, and the task decisions D1 (a), no index and `crit` of the same day).

| # | Change | Reason |
|---|---|---|
| C174 | **SMTP timeout per operation (§9.5 e-mail, C71, C138; SPEC §7.6 v2.50; TD-58, #382).** `EmailSettings` reads the 🔧 `Email:Smtp:Timeout` (`appsettings.json`: `00:00:15`, owner decision O-S6-3) and stops the start without a positive duration of at most `int.MaxValue` milliseconds; `MailKitSmtpTransport` sets it as `SmtpClient.Timeout`, which bounds every network operation — connect, greeting, sign-in and each command of the send. A stalled server ends the attempt with a `TimeoutException`, which `SmtpEmailSender` handles like any failed attempt: with its one retry a request waits at most about twice the timeout instead of four minutes. Tests: `SmtpTimeoutTests` (a fake server on the loopback that stalls before the greeting, at the sign-in or at the send), `EmailSettingsTests`. | A stalled SMTP server held the registration, the confirmation resend and the invitation for minutes (S5-11 P3). |
| C175 | **Clean-up of the account tokens (§9.1, §9.3; C72, C139, C141; SPEC §8.8 v2.50; TD-59, #383).** `AccountTokenCleanupService` (IdentityAccess, `Accounts/`) is a `BackgroundService` like `InvitationCleanupService` (C147): at startup and then every `Account:Tokens:CleanupInterval` (`appsettings.json`: 1 h, an operations setting, not 🔧) on a `PeriodicTimer` over `TimeProvider`, it deletes every row of `identity."AccountTokens"` with `ExpiresAt <= now`, used or not, in one `ExecuteDeleteAsync`. A used row stays until its expiry, so the second click on a confirmation link still answers 204 (SPEC decision S5-12 4 (b)); afterwards every link answers as invalid, as an expired one did before (task decision D1 (a), following the invitations' D7 (a)). The table holds no organisation id, so no query filter is passed and no allow-list entry is needed. **No index on `ExpiresAt` and no migration** (task decision): the clean-up keeps the table to the tokens of one lifetime, so the hourly scan is cheap. Single API instance (§10), like the log retention. Tests: `AccountTokenCleanupTests` (the delete rule around the expiry, and the hosted service on `FakeTimeProvider`), `AccountTokenTests` (the setting). | `identity."AccountTokens"` gained one row per registration, resend and reset request and kept the hashes without end; only an account deletion removed them (S5-12a P4; data minimisation). |
| C176 | **The client's nginx error log without share tokens (§9.5; C158; TD-57, #381).** `CLIENT/CLIENT.ProcureAccess/nginx.conf` sets `error_log /var/log/nginx/error.log crit;` in its `server` block. The error log has no format, so it cannot be masked like the access log (C158): an upstream failure at level `error` (API down, 502/504) wrote `request: "GET /api/v1/shared/<token> …"` and `referrer: "…/geteilt/<token>"` — the referrer also for every other request of the shared page. A rule for the share paths alone would have missed the referrer, so the whole server logs only `crit` and above (failures of nginx itself, which may still quote a request; the residual case is accepted). The status of a failed request stays in the masked access log, its cause in the API's log. The image's main-context error log (startup messages, no request) is unchanged. Proof: a manual check on `nginx:1.30-alpine` with this file, before and after (protocol S6-05 §4). The Traefik/Dokploy access log is outside the repository (TD-28 (9)). | The repository part of S5-23 P1: the client image's log held share tokens on every upstream failure. |

| C168 | **Reload flows on the client as built (§8.1, §8.3; C127; issues #296, #310; TECH_DEBT TD-39; UX copy v40 U161; Handoff 1.34).** (1) *Lost lazy chunk (#296):* the router gets `withNavigationErrorHandler(handleChunkLoadError)` (`core/errors/chunk-reload.ts`). A navigation error is a lost chunk when it is the `TypeError` of a failed dynamic `import()`; the three engines' messages are kept in one list (`CHUNK_LOAD_MESSAGES`), because the engines give no error code. Offline (`ConnectionStatus`) nothing happens and the offline notice stays. Otherwise the target is loaded in full once: `DocumentReload` writes `{ paChunkReload: <router URL> }` through Angular's `Location` into the target's history entry (`go`, or `replaceState` when the address already shows it) and calls `location.reload()` — both in the next task, because the router restores the previous address and state synchronously after the handler returns (`NavigationError` → `restoreHistory`). The browser keeps an entry's state across the reload, so when the same target fails again in that entry the handler returns a `RedirectCommand` to the server page S80 with `skipLocationChange` instead of a second reload. The guard lives in the session history only — never in browser storage (R-CON-09, R-SET-02) and never in the URL. Every other navigation error keeps the router's default. The in-memory draft is lost on the reload, as on any reload. (2) *S13 after `release-mismatch` (#310, completes C127 (4)):* for the draft of S11, S13 offers „Schließen“ and „Kriterien neu laden“, which closes the dialog with `{ reload: true }`. `withConfigurator().reloadCriteriaListForActiveRelease()` drops running requests, search and filter, the pinned release, the options and every per-criterion decision, keeps the four-layer draft, loads the options without a release — their answer pins the active release again (ADR-002) — and then the list; `loadCriteriaList` pins the answer's release when none is pinned. S11 moves the focus to its H1 afterwards. A saved list exports under its own release and keeps „Schließen“ only. `ICatalogReleaseCarryOver` (C155, the copy of a saved list) is not used: the UX copy promises the selection, not the per-criterion changes. | #296: since S4-22 a tab opened before a deployment ends in a dead click. #310: without an action S11 could not get a list it can export after a release change; needed before a second release is activated (TD-39). |

The Configurator module implements both; Workspace joins with its step. The order of C163 (4) is unchanged: begin → the modules' data → share links → complete → the account. The Development rebuild and the E2E seeder keep their guards and test data in the host and call `IdentityAccessModule.RebuildSampleUsersAsync` and `SeedTestUsersAsync` (`SeedUsers`, `TestOrganizationSeeder`). ### Changes from v79

Sprint 05, task S5-30 (`docs/sprints/sprint_05/S5-30_session_warning.md`; owner decisions of 2026-11-17: D1 (b) — at the limit the client asks the probe and the login overlay replaces the warning · D2 (a) — before the absolute limit „Schließen“ with the focus and „Jetzt abmelden“ · D3 (b) — another account in the overlay: no retry, the previous user's data goes, start page · D4 (a) — an E2E clock of our own, test environments only · Q1 (a) — the singular texts of UX copy v39 · Q2 (a) — the clock moves per request with a cookie, not globally).

| # | Change | Reason |
|---|---|---|
| C166 | **Session warning and the completed login overlay as built (§8.3; completes C52, C60, C136, C137, C165; SPEC R-AUTH-06, R-AUTH-07, §8.8 v2.48; TESTING V297 – V301; A11Y-04 `active`).** (1) *Warning (`SessionWarningService`, `core/session/`, started by `App`):* from `sessionExpiresAt`, `sessionAbsoluteExpiresAt` and `sessionWarningLeadSeconds` it plans the warning `warningLeadSeconds` before the earlier limit and a check at that limit, against the browser's clock. Before the warning it asks the probe, which does not renew (another tab may have extended or ended the session); a failed probe still warns (`refreshSession()` of `withIdentity()` leaves the state as it is and answers `unknown`). An answer that moves the limit out of the warning time closes the warning. A limit that has already passed when it is planned shows nothing — the next request answers 401 — so a wrong clock never starts a probe loop. (2) *Dialog (`SessionWarningDialog`, `features/identity/dialogs/`):* `MatDialog` with `role="alertdialog"`, described by its text; idle limit next: `shell.session.warning.body`, „Jetzt abmelden“ and „Angemeldet bleiben“ (focus), which calls `POST /auth/session/extend`, announces `shell.session.extended` and closes, a failure keeps it open; absolute limit next: `.absolute`, „Jetzt abmelden“ and „Schließen“ (focus) (D2 (a)). The minutes come with `translatePlural` (`.one`, UX copy U160) and change at most once a minute without a new announcement; Esc closes without extending. (3) *At the limit (D1 (b)):* the probe again; an ended session closes the warning and opens the login overlay over the page, once per ended session (the warning and the limit may both find it). (4) *Login overlay (completes C60, C137):* before it opens it calls the probe for the antiforgery token of the anonymous state, without applying the answer — after a real expiry the old token belongs to the ended session and the sign-in was refused (found by J5; before S5-30 the E2E test of the overlay faked the 401 for an anonymous user). Closing it without a sign-in reads the session again, so the header shows the user signed out on the same page (R-AUTH-07). Another account than the one of the ended session (D3 (b)): the overlay closes without a retry, `clearUserData()` of the root store drops the previous user's draft, list and legacy data, and the start page shows with the focus on its H1 (`SignOutService`, also used by „Abmelden“ and „Jetzt abmelden“); `shell.session.restored` is announced only for the same account. (5) *E2E clock (D4 (a), Q2 (a); TESTING §3.3):* `TestClock` in the host replaces `TimeProvider` only in Development and E2E with `TestClock__Enabled`, which only `docker-compose.e2e.yml` sets; it adds the offset of the request's cookie `pa-test-clock` (whole seconds, 0 to two days), kept in the request's `HttpContext.Items` and read through `IHttpContextAccessor`, so it applies to that request alone, also in the callbacks when the response starts. No endpoint and no shipped test package (`FakeTimeProvider` stays a test dependency). (6) *Known limits (S5-30 open points):* the client compares server times with the browser's clock; the dev-only API watcher (`src/dev/dev-reload-health.ts`) sends the session cookie to `/health/live` every 5 seconds, which renews the session on the dev server (nginx forwards only `/api/`); the settings read after a sign-in announces „Wird geladen …“ right after `shell.session.restored`. (7) *No migration, no new dependency.* | Migration step 4, M4.2; owner decisions S5-30 |

### Changes from v78

Sprint 05, task S5-29 (`docs/sprints/sprint_05/S5-29_session_hardening.md`; owner decisions of 2026-11-17: 1a — the client reads the expiry headers in this task · 2a — `Session:WarningLead` is a server setting the probe serves · 3a — a signed-in read of the catalog is not stored · 4a — Identity's security-stamp validator with a 🔧 interval of zero).

| # | Change | Reason |
|---|---|---|
| C165 | **Session hardening as built (§7.1, §8.3, §9.1; completes C52, C60, C133, C136; SPEC R-AUTH-05, §8.8 v2.47; TESTING V293 – V296; SEC-07, SEC-08 `active`).** (1) *Validation (`SessionCookieEvents.ValidatePrincipal`):* first the limits — a ticket whose idle or absolute limit is reached is rejected and the cookie deleted (the cookie handler alone still accepts a ticket in the instant of its expiry); then Identity's security-stamp validator; then the renewal. (2) *Renewal:* every authenticated request except the probe renews to min(now + `Session:IdleTimeout`, sign-in time + `Session:AbsoluteTimeout`). The handler renews by the ticket's own length (expiry minus issue time), so the event sets both and asks for the renewal; `CheckSlidingExpiration` always answers no, so the handler's rule (after half of the window) never applies. The probe never renews, although the stamp validator asks for a renewal whenever it checked. (3) *Extend:* `POST /api/v1/auth/session/extend` (IdentityAccess, signed in, antiforgery, no rate limit) answers 204 and does nothing itself — it is an authenticated request, so (2) renews. C60's „sets `ExpiresUtc` explicitly and signs in again with the existing `Items`“ is not needed for the same behaviour; the sign-in time stays because a renewal keeps the ticket's items. (4) *Expiry headers:* `X-Session-Expires-At` and `X-Session-Absolute-Expires-At` (ISO 8601, UTC, whole seconds as the ticket stores them) on every answer to an authenticated request — module endpoints, legacy controllers, error answers, the probe (its unrenewed limits) — and on the answer of the sign-in; a sign-out, also the one of a rejected session, removes them. (5) *Security stamp (decision 4a):* 🔧 `Session:SecurityStampValidationInterval` (00:00:00) is the validator's interval, so the stamp is read on every request: a completed password reset and an account deletion end the user's other sessions with their next request (SEC-08). The validator also rebuilds the principal from the database each time, so a changed role applies with the next request; the price is the user, role and claim reads on every signed-in request (accepted at the expected load, §11.1, as the membership read of C144). S61 has no password change (decision S5-28 1a), so „password change“ is the reset. (6) *Warning lead (decision 2a):* 🔧 `Session:WarningLead` (00:05:00; positive and shorter than the idle limit, or the start stops) is served by the probe as `warningLeadSeconds` for a signed-in user; the client keeps no copy. (7) *Caching (decision 3a):* an answer that renews the cookie leaves with `Cache-Control: no-cache,no-store`, which the cookie handler sets; a signed-in read of the catalog is therefore never stored, an anonymous one keeps the catalog's header (C107, §9.6). No personal header reaches a shared cache. (8) *Client (decision 1a; §8.3 job 2):* `authInterceptor` writes the two headers of every answer, also a failed one, into `SessionLimits` (`core/session/session-limits.ts`, a service without imports: the interceptor must not inject the root store, whose first request runs while it is built); `withIdentity()` sets the limits from the probe, shows them as `sessionExpiresAt` and `sessionAbsoluteExpiresAt` only while somebody is signed in, and holds `sessionWarningLeadSeconds`. Nothing shows them yet: the warning dialog is S5-30 (M4.2). (9) *No migration, no new dependency.* | Migration step 4, M4.1; owner decisions S5-29 1a – 4a |

### Changes from v76

Sprint 05, task S5-28a (`docs/sprints/sprint_05/S5-28a_account_deletion_server.md`; owner decisions of 2026-11-17: 1a — S61 gets no change of e-mail address or password · 2a — the account row stays soft-deleted, the password hash is cleared · 3 — the parts of R-AUTH-10 on contributor profiles, entries, reports and projects join with their steps · 4 — the other sessions end at the next check of the security stamp until S5-29 · 5a — the legacy proposal list goes with the profile page · 6a — 409 `sole-owner` names every organisation · P1 — split into S5-28a server and S5-28b client · P2 (a) — the request has no body · P3 (a) — no message after the deletion, as the sign-out).

| # | Change | Reason |
|---|---|---|
| C163 | **Account deletion on the server as built (§5.3, §7.2, §9.3; C79, C80, C151, C158; SPEC R-AUTH-10, §8.6, §8.13 v2.45; TESTING V286 – V288).** (1) *Endpoint:* `DELETE /api/v1/account` in the host (`API/Account/AccountDeletionEndpoints.cs`), because the account is a legacy `User` until M3.1. Signed in (401), antiforgery, no confirmed address, no rate limit (SPEC §7.3 names none), **no body** (owner decision P2 (a); §7.2 had planned `{ emailConfirmation }`): the address typed on S61 guards against a slip in the client, the antiforgery token against a forged request. 204 ends the session like the sign-out (cookie deleted, new antiforgery token). (2) *Rule (TEN-11, decision 6a):* the only Owner of an organisation with other members gets 409 Problem Details `sole-owner` (new type, `ProblemTypes.SoleOwner`) with the extension `organizations`, the names of all such organisations by name (German collation); nothing changes. An organisation whose only member the user is ends with the account. (3) *Contracts:* `IAccountDeletion` (`IdentityAccess.Contracts/Accounts`; `AccountDeletionService`) with `BeginAsync` — the check, and the open invitations of the organisations that end are deleted, so nobody joins them meanwhile — and `CompleteAsync` — the check again, then these organisations (memberships and invitations cascade), the user's other memberships, the `UserPreferences` row and the account tokens. Both run in one transaction under the row locks of the user's organisations (`OrganizationRowLock`, taken in the order of the ids), read and delete through the organisation query filter and need no bypass (TEN-01, TEN-02). `ICriteriaListDeletion` (`Configurator.Contracts/Accounts`) deletes the lists of the given organisations with their rows (cascade); `IShareLinks.DeleteOfOrganizationsAsync` their share links; both through the filter of the request, which still holds the organisations. (4) *Order without a shared transaction (ADR-014):* begin → lists → share links → complete → the account. Each step can be repeated, so a failure in between answers 500, the account still signs in, and the next attempt finishes; no order leaves rows nobody can reach. (5) *The account (decision 2a):* the row stays soft-deleted (§9.3 never hard-deletes it) with the address and user name `deleted-<guid>@deleted.invalid` (reserved domain, RFC 2606; the legacy deletion wrote `@anonymous.de`), `EmailConfirmed` false, `PasswordHash` null, `DeletedAt` from `TimeProvider`, and a new security stamp, so the other sessions end at its next check (immediate end: SEC-08, S5-29; decision 4). (6) *Deleted list:* `DELETE /configurations/{id}` now deletes the list's share links too (`IShareLinks.DeleteOfResourceAsync`, after the list; S5-23 R5) — before, the rows stayed and answered 404 (S5-23 8). (7) *Not built (decision 3):* contributor profiles and their links, published entries, experience reports (steps 6 – 7; PRV-06, PRV-10) and procurement projects (step 5) join the deletion with their steps. Left as they are: open invitations other organisations sent to the deleted address (they expire and are cleaned up, C147), `CreatedByUserId` of lists in organisations that live on, and the legacy proposals of the user. (8) *Legacy:* `DELETE /api/Users` stays until S5-28b removes the legacy profile page that calls it; S61 with the download of the export and the deletion dialog is S5-28b | S5-28a; SPEC R-AUTH-10; TEN-11 |

Sprint 05, task S5-26 (`docs/sprints/sprint_05/S5-26_settings_s60.md`; owner decisions of 2026-11-17: D1 (a) — „Deutsch“ as the one chosen option of „Sprache“, new UX key `settings.language.de` (UX copy v37 U157) · D2 (a) — a change is saved at once; a failed save shows the system states of the error interceptor, the choice stays applied for the session and is saved with the next change).

| # | Change | Reason |
|---|---|---|
| C162 | **Settings page S60 as built (§5.2, §8; C73, C160, C161; SPEC R-SET-01, R-SET-04, §8.12; Handoff §5.3; TESTING V283 – V285).** (1) *Route:* `/einstellungen` (`SETTINGS_PATH` in `features/settings/models/user-preferences.ts`), lazy `settings.routing.ts` with `authGuard` and a resolver that reads the settings again through `withSettings.readPreferences()` (`PreferencesApiService.read()`, `CALLER_SHOWS_FAILURES`) with the loading bar, so the page shows what is stored even when the read after the sign-in failed and a change never saves the defaults over the stored values; a failed read shows the S80 server text in place (`SettingsLoad`, as S14 and S21). „Einstellungen“ in the menu „Konto“ and in the compact menu, after „Kriterienlisten“ (UX copy U138, U140). (2) *Page:* `features/settings/pages/settings/` — H1, intro, one card with four fieldsets of radio groups in the order of the Handoff: „Sprache“ (only „Deutsch“, hint), „Erscheinungsbild“, „Bewegung und Animationen“, „Kontrast“ (only the system option until H2); no save button. (3) *Saving (D2 (a)):* `withSettings.changePreferences(next)` applies the four values at once through `AppearanceService` (without reload) and sends `PUT /api/v1/account/preferences` (`PreferencesApiService.save()`, unmarked) after the save still in flight, so the last change is the one stored; the page announces `settings.saved` only when the newest change is stored. A failure gets the interceptor's system states (offline notice, S80, snackbar of the problem type) and the choice stays applied. One counter orders reads, sign-outs and changes: only the newest counts, so an answer for a user who signed out is dropped, a read never undoes a later change, and a slower read after the sign-in never replaces S60's read. Nothing in browser storage (R-SET-02). | S5-26 |

Sprint 05, task S5-23 (`docs/sprints/sprint_05/S5-23_share_links_api.md`; owner decisions of 2026-11-16: S5-23 1a — the token stays in the path, the log sinks and the access log mask it · 2a — the expiry is a calendar date, the link works until the end of that day in Europe/Berlin · 3a — creating and revoking need a confirmed address · 4a — `GET …/share-links` for Editor and Owner · 5 — the table in Platform's context, not Configurator's · 6 — no `Scope` column · 7 — no rate limit · 8 — a deleted list's links answer 404, their rows stay · 9 — „a project copy → 409“ with step 5 · a — the shared list in the shape of `GET /configurations/{id}`).

|---|---|---|
| C158 | **Share links as built (§4.3, §5.3, §7.2, §9.5; C59, C81; SPEC R-SAV-04, R-SAV-05, SD15, §8.5, §8.10 v2.40; TESTING V269 – V271).** (1) *Model (S5-23 5, 6):* `ShareLink` (`Id`, `OrganizationId`, `TokenHash` (SHA-256, unique), `ResourceType` (`Configuration`), `ResourceId`, `CreatedAt`, `ExpiresAt`, `RevokedAt?`) in table `sharing."ShareLinks"` of `PlatformDbContext` (migration `AddShareLinks`), without `Scope` and without creator; no foreign key leaves the schema. `PlatformDbContext` now takes `IOrganizationContext` and filters the links like every scoped entity (TEN-01); a context outside a request passes `FixedOrganizationContext.None`, and the `catalog` command host, which has no IdentityAccess module, registers it. (2) *Contracts (`Platform.Contracts/Sharing`):* `IShareLinks` (create, list active, find, revoke; through the filter) for the owning module and `IShareableResourceProvider` (`ReadSharedAsync(resourceId, expiresAt)`) implemented by it; Platform depends on no module. (3) *Management (Configurator, `Sharing/ShareLinkEndpoints`):* `POST /configurations/{id}/share-links` `{ expiresOn }` → 201 `{ id, token, expiresAt }`, `GET /configurations/{id}/share-links` → `[ { id, createdAt, expiresAt } ]` (active only, never a token; S5-23 4a), `DELETE /share-links/{id}` → 204 (also when already revoked). The organisation comes from the stored list or link through the filter (404), then Editor on it (403 Reader); creating and revoking need `ConfirmedEmail` (S5-23 3a) and antiforgery. The endpoints are Configurator's because the role check needs `IdentityAccess.Contracts`, which Platform may not use. (4) *Expiry (S5-23 2a; 🔧 `ShareLinks:MaxExpiryMonths` 12, `ShareLinks:TimeZone` Europe/Berlin, checked at start):* `expiresOn` is a date from tomorrow to today plus 12 months, both in Berlin; `ExpiresAt` is the following midnight in Berlin, in UTC; otherwise 400 `validation` at `expiresOn`. The 90-day default is the client's (S15). (5) *Anonymous read (Platform, `Sharing/SharedEndpoints`):* `GET /api/v1/shared/{token}` without sign-in or rate limit (S5-23 7); a token that is not 43 Base64url characters, an unknown, revoked or expired one and a deleted list (S5-23 8) give the same 404 `not-found`. The lookup by hash and Configurator's read of the list are the two TEN-02 call sites of the anonymous path. The answer (S5-23 a) is `{ name, releaseVersion, expiresAt, selection, criteria }` in the shape of `GET /configurations/{id}` without `id`, `organizationId`, `createdAt`, `modifiedAt`; S16 joins the texts from `GET /catalog/criteria` of the release as S11 does; `no-store` from the platform default. (6) *Logs (S5-23 1a):* the Serilog enricher `ShareTokenMaskingEnricher` replaces the token in every text property matching `/api/v…/shared/<token>` (any case) by `***` before a sink writes the event — the request scope's `RequestPath`, the hosting events' `Path`, and so the rendered message; the client's nginx access log masks `/api/v1/shared/…` and `/geteilt/…` in the path and the referrer. nginx's error log (upstream failures) and the proxy in front (Traefik) are outside this. (7) *Not yet:* the project copy's 409 (step 5, C53); deleting a list leaves its link rows (S5-23 8) | S5-23: share links of M3.5 (SPRINT_05 S5-23); the token is a link meant to be passed on, so it stays a plain URL (D7) |

### Changes from v68

Sprint 05, task S5-21a (`docs/sprints/sprint_05/S5-21a_edit_delete_server.md`; owner decisions of 2026-11-16: S5-21 1a — S5-21 is split, S5-21a builds the server part, S5-21b the screens and J1 · 2a — no ETag/If-Match on saved lists until API-03 in step 5 · 3a — `PUT …/selection` · 4a — the decisions a selection change drops are named · 5a — on a list of an older release the criteria stay editable, the selection not · 6a — a copy keeps the name · 7a — delete on S14 · 8b — a neutral text instead of „Von Ihnen geändert“ (S5-21b) · a — a weight without a chosen level is carried as „B chosen“; the defaults of the plan: every change of a list needs a confirmed address, Readers may export, the slug rule).

| # | Change | Reason |
|---|---|---|
| C155 | **Changing, deleting, copying and exporting a saved list as built, server part (§4.3, §4.4 Flow A2, §5.3, §7.2, §12 step 3; C58, C153; SPEC R-SAV-03, R-SAV-06, R-LST-12, R-LST-14, §7.3, §8.3, §8.10 v2.37; TESTING V255 – V262).** (1) *Endpoints* (Configurator, `ConfigurationEndpoints`): `PATCH /configurations/{id}/criteria/{criterionRevisionId}` replaces the whole decision on one criterion (the rules of a saved row: level name, 🔧 weight range and B only, note ≤ 1 000), 204, also on a list of an older release (S5-21 5a); `PUT /configurations/{id}/selection` `{ selection }` (S5-21 3a, new in §7.2) replaces the selection under the list's release, only while that release is active (else 400 `release-mismatch`), checked like a new list (CAT-11, CAT-12, CAT-18), and answers the criteria whose decisions left with the old selection (S5-21 4a); `DELETE /configurations/{id}` (204; rows deleted with the list; share links join with S5-23, project copies are separate lists, D1); `POST /configurations/{id}/copies` `{ release: "active" }` (201 with `droppedValues` and `notCarried`, `Location` of the new list; 400 `validation` at `release` for anything but `active` or a list already on the active release). Each takes the organisation from the stored list through the filter (404 non-member, TEN-01) and needs Editor on it (403 Reader, TEN-05), a confirmed address (SEC-10) and antiforgery. (2) *Recommendations:* every row of a list keeps the recommendation it was saved with (CAT-08); a selection change sets it anew for the new selection (`ConfigurationCriterion.Reset`) and is the only path that does. `CriteriaConfiguration.ChangeSelection` keeps the value and criterion rows that stay as the same objects, so EF updates them instead of deleting and inserting the same keys. (3) *Carrying decisions over* (`DecisionCarryOver`, for the selection change and the copy): included or not, chosen level, weight and note move as they are under the new recommendation; a chosen level equal to it counts as no choice; a weight without a chosen level is kept as „B chosen“ when the new recommendation is not B (owner decision S5-21 a). Reported are only rows with a decision (`HasDecision`: excluded, level, weight or note). (4) *Copy (D6, C58, CAT-22):* `Catalog.Contracts` gets `ICatalogReleaseCarryOver.CarryOverAsync(fromReleaseVersion, selection)` (Catalog `Criteria/CatalogReleaseCarryOver`): the selection under its release and under the active release, the dropped values by German name in layer order — an inactive use case first (CAT-18), then, going down the layers, every value a kept higher-layer value excludes (exclusions point downwards, CAT-13) — and for each old criterion the revision that continues it, by key or by `SupersededByKey` followed across releases (read from the database, not the release cache, because a later import sets it). Of several old criteria continued by one (a merge) the one with its own key wins, then one with a decision; the others with a decision are reported. The copy has the same organisation and name (S5-21 6a), the copier as creator, the active release; the source list is unchanged (ADR-002). If nothing complete is left, 400 `selection-excluded`. (5) *Saved export* (Export, `Http/SavedExportEndpoint`): `POST /configurations/{id}/exports` `{ format }` through `PersistedConfigurationSource` for every member (Readers too), `no-store`, usage event `ExportCreated` signed in, rate limit `SavedExport` per user 60 / 10 min 🔧 (SPEC §7.3); the file name gets the slug of the list name after „Kriterien“ (`ExportFileWriter.SlugOf`: umlauts and ß spelled out, accents dropped, other characters a hyphen, at most 50, case kept; SPEC §8.3). (6) *No ETag/If-Match* (S5-21 2a): API-03 for saved lists comes with step 5 and S37; until then the last change wins. No EF migration. (7) *Not yet:* the screens, „Auswahl ändern“ on the client, the delete dialog, the older-release notice with „Neue Liste mit derselben Auswahl“ and the J1 file comparison (S5-21b) | S5-21a; owner decisions S5-21 1a – 8b, a |

### Changes from v66

Sprint 05, task S5-20a (`docs/sprints/sprint_05/S5-20a_save_server.md`; owner decisions of 2026-11-16: S5-20 1a — S5-20 is split, S5-20a builds the endpoints, S5-20b the screens and J1 · A (a′) — one draft check for the export and saving, in Configurator, with the ARC-03 rules of the ad-hoc source narrowed to it · B (a) — saving and listing name their organisation in the body or the query).

|---|---|---|
| C153 | **Saved-list endpoints and the shared draft check as built (§4.4 Flow A, §7.2, §9.1, §12 step 3; C62, C123, C152; TESTING V244 – V248).** (1) *One draft check (decision A (a′)):* `IConfigurationDraftCheck.CheckAsync(CriteriaDraft, DraftPurpose)` in `Configurator.Contracts/Drafts` with plain records (`DraftSelection`, `DraftCriterion` — unknown JSON members refused —, `DraftCheckResult` with an outcome `Checked`/`Invalid`/`SelectionExcluded`/`ReleaseNotFound`/`ReleaseNotActive`, `CheckedDraft`, `CheckedCriterion`); Configurator implements it (`Drafts/ConfigurationDraftCheck`) with the rules and messages of the ad-hoc export moved unchanged (C123 (4)), and owns the 🔧 weight range (`Drafts/PriorityWeightRange`, `Configurator:PriorityWeight`, read at start by `AddConfiguratorModule`). The contract has no problem type names, because a contracts project references no `Platform.Contracts` (ADR-017); each caller maps the outcome. `AdHocSelectionSource` maps it to the export's Problem Details and builds the rows of the included criteria — its answers are unchanged. (2) *Purpose:* the only rule that differs — a new list refuses an inactive use case (`DraftPurpose.Save`, 400 `validation` at `selection.useCaseTemplateId`, CAT-18), the export accepts it (owner, S3-20). For that `ResolvedCatalogSelection` (`Catalog.Contracts`) carries `UseCaseIsActive`. (3) *Module rule (§4.4):* the ad-hoc source uses `Catalog.Contracts` and, of `Configurator.Contracts`, only the draft check — never a saved configuration, which is what C62 keeps out. ARC-03 checks it: the ad-hoc source uses no type of `Configurator.Contracts` outside `Drafts`; of Export only `PersistedConfigurationSource` uses the other types and only the ad-hoc source the draft check. §4.3 is unchanged: Export already lists `Configurator.Contracts`. (4) *Endpoints (§7.2):* minimal APIs of the Configurator module (`Configurations/ConfigurationEndpoints`, `ConfigurationService`; the module needs no legacy user, so they are not in the host), every one with a sign-in (401). `POST /api/v1/configurations` `{ organizationId, name, selection, criteria[] }` — the draft in the shape of the anonymous export —, gate `ConfirmedEmail` (SEC-10), antiforgery (SEC-02), body ≤ 1 MB like the export (SPEC §7.2), no rate limit (SPEC §7.3 lists none): the role check on `organizationId` comes first (Editor; 404 non-member, 403 Reader, TEN-05), then the name (trimmed, required, ≤ 120) and the draft check (Save); the name's and the draft's field errors come in one answer; 201 `{ id }` with `Location: /api/v1/configurations/{id}`; the recommended level of each row is the one the catalog resolved, never the client's (EXP-07, CAT-08). `GET /api/v1/configurations/{id}`: through the organisation filter, so another organisation's list is 404 (TEN-01); every member reads; the answer holds name, organisation, release, `createdAt`, `modifiedAt`, the selection by key (`useCaseTemplateId`, `appTypeIds` … as the request) and one decision per criterion by revision id (`resolvedLevel`, `overrideLevel`, `overrideWeight`, `note`; levels `A`/`B`/`Info`) — texts and order come from `GET /catalog/criteria?release=<version>`, which serves a stored older release too. `GET /api/v1/configurations?organizationId=` (Reader and up) for S14: `[ { id, name, releaseVersion, createdAt, modifiedAt } ]`, the most recently changed first, without paging and without the creator (owner decision S5-20 3a); the client sorts. (5) *Organisation (decision B (a)):* named in the body or the query because the routes are flat (§7.2), always checked as a resource with `OrganizationRoleRequirement` on `OrganizationReference`; never a header; a read by id takes it from the stored list. (6) *Not yet:* `Cache-Control: no-store` on these answers with the TEN-04 workspace part (S5-22); `PATCH`, delete and the saved export (S5-21); the weight range served with the API (S5-22, TD-45). No EF migration | S5-20a; owner decisions S5-20 1a, A (a′), B (a); SPEC R-SAV-01, R-SAV-02 |

### Changes from v65

Sprint 05, task S5-19 (`docs/sprints/sprint_05/S5-19_configurator_persistence.md`; owner decisions of 2026-11-16: S5-19 1a — O-22 / O-S5-5: a contracts project may reference the contracts of the modules its row lists · 2a — the lists name the release by version and the values by key · 3a — no endpoint in this task · 4 — shape and scope of the criteria lists in the personal data export, and `CreatedAt` / `ModifiedAt` on the list).

| # | Change | Reason |
|---|---|---|
| C152 | **Configurator persistence as built (§4.3, §4.4, §5.3, §12 step 3, §16.5, ADR-017; C62, C151; TESTING V239 – V243).** (1) *Contracts → contracts (O-22 decided, ADR-017):* a `Contracts` project may reference the `Contracts` projects of the modules its row of §4.3 lists, and nothing else — not `Platform.Contracts`, not a module project; along the table there is no cycle. ARC-03 checks the references, the types and the table. First use: `Configurator.Contracts` → `Catalog.Contracts` for `PriorityLevel`. (2) *Tables:* the Configurator module has its own context `ConfiguratorDbContext` in schema `configurator` (ADR-014; migration `InitialConfiguratorSchema`): `CriteriaConfigurations` (random `Guid` id, `OrganizationId`, `Name` ≤ 120, `ReleaseVersion`, `UseCaseKey?`, `CreatedByUserId`, `CreatedAt`, `ModifiedAt`) and its row tables `ConfigurationAppTypes`, `ConfigurationProductParts`, `ConfigurationTestTypes` (list + value key) and `ConfigurationCriteria` (list + `CriterionRevisionId`; `IsIncluded`, `ResolvedLevel`, `OverrideLevel?`, `OverrideWeight?`, `NoteDe` ≤ 1 000). (3) *Version and keys instead of ids (decision 2a):* the release is named by its version and a filter value by its key, as `Catalog.Contracts` and the API name them; `CriterionRevisionId` is the id the contract already carries. No foreign key leaves the schema: the catalog, the organisations and the users live in other contexts. (4) *Aggregate:* the row tables are owned collections of the list — stored, loaded and deleted with it, without a set of their own — so the organisation filter on `CriteriaConfiguration` (ADR-007, TEN-01) covers them. `CriteriaConfiguration.Create` takes a selection the catalog has resolved and one draft per criterion; `Decide` stores a decision and stamps `ModifiedAt`; `ResolvedLevel` is set once (CAT-08); a level equal to the recommendation counts as no choice. (5) *Reader:* `IConfigurationReader.FindAsync(id)` in `Configurator.Contracts` returns the list (name, release, keys, decisions) through the filter of the request, `null` for a list that does not exist for it. (6) *Export source:* `PersistedConfigurationSource` in Export reads the list through the reader and the texts and recommendations of the pinned release through `ICatalogSelectionResolver`, also when a newer release is active (CAT-06); both sources build a row with `ExportRow.Of` (EXP-06). A list unknown to the request is 404 `not-found`. (7) *No endpoint (decision 3a):* `POST /api/v1/configurations`, the reads and the list follow with S5-20, `PATCH` and `POST /api/v1/configurations/{id}/exports` with S5-21; the weight range of 🔧 `Configurator:PriorityWeight` and the check of a selection against its release belong to the save path. (8) *Personal data export (C151 (3), decision 4):* `ICriteriaListDataExport` in `Configurator.Contracts` gives the lists whose `CreatedByUserId` is the user, read through the filter of the request, so for the organisations the user still belongs to; per list name, organisation name, release, creation time, the selection by name and per criterion number, title, inclusion, recommended (stored) and effective level, weight and note; no ids. `ProcurementProjectId` (C53) follows in step 5 | S5-19; O-S5-5; TESTING CAT-08, EXP-06, ARC-03 |

(4) *Creation time* (decision 2a): `User.CreatedAt` (`timestamptz`, nullable; legacy migration `AddUserCreatedAt`), stamped from `TimeProvider` by `ClockedUserManager.CreateAsync`, so registration and the E2E seeder set it; accounts from before S5-27 export `null`. (5) *Out of scope* (decision 3a): the legacy proposals a user made (`Proposals.ProposerId`) and open invitations to the user's address are not exported. (6) *Pseudonymity* (owner decision of 2026-11-16): the export is the one exception to „no endpoint returns both the account and the profile“ (§9.3, PRV-04 / TESTING V238) — only the user's own profiles, without ids or `UserContributorLink` rows. | S5-27; SPEC R-AUTH-09, §8.4, SD14 |
