### Changes from v26

Sprint 03, task S3-24 (`docs/sprints/sprint_03/S3-24_data_protection_keys.md`; owner decisions 1a, 2 and 3a of 2026-11-09).

| # | Change | Driver |
|---|---|---|
| C112 | **Data Protection key store as built ahead of step 3 (§4.3, §9.1, §10.4; part of C60).** (1) **Owner (decision 1a):** the IdentityAccess module, as §4.3 lists it, not Platform: `IdentityAccessDbContext` (`internal`, schema `identity`, history `identity."__EFMigrationsHistory"`, migration `InitialIdentityAccessSchema`) is the module's first context (ADR-014); it maps only `DataProtectionKeys` until step 3 adds users, memberships and contributor profiles. `AddIdentityAccessModule(configuration)` registers it, its `IModuleDatabaseMigrator` (after Platform, before Catalog) and `AddDataProtection().SetApplicationName("ProcureAccess").PersistKeysToDbContext<IdentityAccessDbContext>()`. The fixed application name replaces the default (the content root path), so hosts started from different folders share the key ring. (2) **Dependency (decision 2):** `Microsoft.AspNetCore.DataProtection.EntityFrameworkCore` 10.0.14 (MIT, Microsoft; D12). (3) **No encryption at rest (decision 3a):** the key XML is stored as written; whoever reads the database or a `pg_dump` reads the keys, and ASP.NET Core logs the warning „No XML encryptor configured“ each time it creates a key (every 90 days). Protecting the keys with a certificate is left for `TECHNICAL.md` before the pilot. *Superseded by C131 (v45, S4-26): the key ring is encrypted with a certificate.* (4) **Effect today:** the legacy Identity token providers (`AddDefaultTokenProviders`: e-mail confirmation, password reset, e-mail change) already use Data Protection, so their links now survive a new container; antiforgery and the session cookie follow in step 3; keys of the old file-system ring (`/home/app/.aspnet/DataProtection-Keys`, locally `~/.aspnet/…`) are not read any more, so a link sent before the deployment fails once. (5) **OPS-04 stays planned** until the cookie session; an integration test without invariant tag shows that a second host on the same database reads what the first protected and a host on another database does not | S3-24, #186; `TECH_DEBT_v2.md` TD-21 |

### Changes from v25

Sprint 03, task S3-23 (`docs/sprints/sprint_03/S3-23_usage_events_fire_and_forget.md`; owner decisions D1 (A), D2, D3 and D4 of 2026-11-09).

| # | Change | Driver |
|---|---|---|
| C111 | **Usage events written fire-and-forget (§5.7; replaces the write path of C91).** (1) **Request path (D1 A, D4):** `IUsageEventWriter` has `void` methods (`CriteriaListShown`, `ExportCreated`); they build the event with the UTC day of the call and put it into a bounded in-memory queue (`System.Threading.Channels`, part of .NET) without waiting, and never throw. (2) **Background service:** `UsageEventBackgroundWriter` (Platform, hosted service) stores the queue in batches of at most 100 through `UsageEventStore`, a `PlatformDbContext` with options of its own **without** the retrying execution strategy of the module's registration. (3) **Retry without duplicates (D2):** the store opens the connection first; if that fails, nothing was sent, so the service keeps the batch and tries again every `UsageEvents:RetryDelay` (on `TimeProvider`) for as long as it takes. If the insert was sent and failed, the batch is logged as lost and not repeated, because the database may have committed it — an event can be lost but is never counted twice. An unexpected exception drops only that batch, so counting never stops the host. (4) **Bounded memory (D3):** `UsageEvents:QueueCapacity` (1000) events wait in the queue; beyond it new events are dropped. The outage, the recovery and a full queue are each logged once, and the number of dropped events after the next write. (5) **Shutdown:** the queue takes no further events, the queued ones are stored until the host's shutdown timeout, and what is left is logged as lost. The queue is per process; like the log retention it assumes a single API instance (§10). No new dependency | #115 (R-MET-01 „fire-and-forget with error log“); S1-23 R1/P4; owner D1–D4 |

### Changes from v24

Sprint 03, task S3-21 (`docs/sprints/sprint_03/S3-21_criteria_suggestion_link.md`; owner decisions 1a, 2a, 3a and 4 of 2026-11-09).

| # | Change | Driver |
|---|---|---|
| C110 | **Criteria-suggestion channel as built (C85, §9.7, §16.4.2).** (1) **Where it lives (decision 2a):** `Contact:CriteriaSuggestion` (SPEC R-SUG-01) is the client setting `criteriaSuggestion` in `environment.ts` / `environment.prod.ts`, read through the token `CRITERIA_SUGGESTION_CHANNEL` (`core/contact/criteria-suggestion.ts`). No endpoint and no API setting (§16.4.2 option C); a build-time value is enough because the channel is WERTE.IT's, the same in every environment, so the client image stays one image. The value is `mailto:<address>` (no query: the subject comes from the UX copy) or an https URL; any other value throws when the footer is created. (2) **Placeholder (decision 3a):** until O-6 the value is `mailto:kriterium-vorschlagen@example.invalid`; `.invalid` is reserved (RFC 2606) and can never receive mail, so it marks every placeholder. The link is shown with it outside production. (3) **Release check instead of a failing build (decision 1a):** the release build runs in CI stage 2, in the client image (E2E) and in staging, so it cannot fail while O-6 is open. `scripts/check-release-config.mjs` fails while the setting of `environment.prod.ts` is the placeholder or not an accepted form; it runs before a production release (there is no release pipeline yet, §10.3), and its self-test runs in CI stage 1. GP.3 adds the legal pages (R-SHL-07) to the same check. (4) **Footer (decision 4):** the link `shell.footer.suggest` comes first in the footer; a visually hidden suffix `suggest.external` (mail) or `suggest.external.url` (URL) names where it leads; the mail gets the subject `suggest.mailSubject`, no body (the body with the criterion key comes with S11, M2.6) | SPEC v2.5-1 – v2.5-3; UX copy v16 U85 |

### Changes from v23

Sprint 03, task S3-20b (`docs/sprints/sprint_03/S3-20b_criteria_endpoint.md`; owner decisions 2, 3 and 7 of 2026-11-09, the defaults stated before S3-20 and not objected to).

| # | Change | Driver |
|---|---|---|
| C109 | **Criteria endpoint as built (§5.1, §7.2, §9.6).** (1) **`GET /api/v1/catalog/criteria`** is a minimal API of the Catalog module like filter-options (C107), with the same `release` rule, the same HTTP caching (one helper, `Http/CatalogJsonAnswer`) and the selection check and priority resolution of C108. (2) **Release content in the cache:** `CatalogReleaseSnapshot` holds each criterion revision with its rules, titles, standard references and procedure texts, the exclusion rules, the app type → procedure mapping and the release's gaps; the reader loads the release once with split queries and caches it without expiry (§9.6). (3) **Complete releases only (decision 7):** the gaps are those `catalog activate` refuses (`Releases/ReleaseCompleteness`, one function for both); a named release with gaps answers 404 `not-found`, so no answer carries a priority without explanation (CAT-02). (4) **Which criteria:** every requirement criterion that applies to a selected app type with no product part or a selected one, and the procedure criterion of each selected test type (CAT-16), in list order (`SortOrder`, then key). (5) **Texts and ratings (C31 as written):** the criterion's procedure texts of the test types the release maps to a *selected* app type (CAT-21), in test-type order; `allowedRatings` is their union, all six levels when none applies, and empty for a procedure criterion (decision 3). (6) **`productPart` (decision 2):** of the applicability rows that match the selection, the selected part with the lowest sort order; null when none names a part. (7) **`appTypesWithoutCriteria[]`:** the selected app types to which no requirement criterion applies under the whole selection, product parts included (CAT-20). (8) **Strings in the answer:** enum values are mapped to strings in the module (camelCase; `level` `A`/`B`/`Info` like SPEC §8.2 and the UX keys), so the JSON does not depend on a serializer setting. The revision's own `RationaleDe` and `Category` are not sent: SPEC §8.2 does not list them and no screen or export column uses them | Owner, S3-20 decisions 2, 3, 7; SPEC_v2.4 |

### Changes from v22

Sprint 03, task S3-20a (`docs/sprints/sprint_03/S3-20a_priority_resolution.md`; owner decisions 1–7 of 2026-11-09 before the task, all as proposed: decision 4 splits S3-20 into S3-20a and S3-20b; decisions 1, 5, 6 and 7 are recorded below; decisions 2 and 3 on the response shape follow with S3-20b).

| # | Change | Driver |
|---|---|---|
| C108 | **Priority resolution and selection check as built (§5.1, §7.2).** (1) **Per app type (decision 1):** each selected app type the criterion applies to — an applicability row names it and either no product part or a selected one — runs the whole chain on its own, most specific first: (app type, use case) → (app type, –) → (–, use case) → (–, –); without a use case only rules 2 and 4. The strictest result of these app types wins: level (A > B > Info), then the higher weight (a rule without weight ranks below one with), then the lower `AppType.SortOrder`. So a selected app type never gets a looser priority than its own chain gives; with Web + Software (offen) and no use case, a rule (Software (offen), –) = Info does not lower Web's catalog default B. A selected app type the criterion does not apply to takes no part. `matchedRule` names the app type for rules 1 and 2; rules 3 and 4 name none. (2) **Procedure criteria** have no applicability (C30), so only rules 3 and 4 apply to them. (3) **No guessing:** if the chain of an app type finds no rule, the criterion has no catalog default and the release is incomplete; the resolution gives no result, and the criteria endpoint answers 404 for such a release (decision 7, S3-20b). (4) **Selection check (CAT-11):** a complete selection has at most one use case, at least one app type, every product part with `IsAlwaysSelected` and any test types, each by a known key; otherwise 400 `validation`, one error per field as the client sends it (`useCase`, `appTypeIds`, `productPartIds`, `appTypeIds[1]`). Only a complete selection is checked against the exclusion rules of the release; a value that a selected higher-layer value excludes gives 400 **`selection-excluded`** with the same field-keyed `errors` (decision 5; SPEC §8.6 named the type without a use until now). (5) **Inactive use cases** are accepted by the criteria selection too and their rules apply (decision 6, as in filter-options, CAT-18): the public endpoint cannot tell a new selection from an old one, and filter-options does not offer them. Code: `src/ProcureAccess.Modules.Catalog/Criteria/` (`CriteriaSelectionReader`, `CriterionRules`, `PriorityResolver`), pure and `internal`; the endpoint follows with S3-20b | Owner, S3-20 decisions 1, 5–7 |

### Changes from v21

Sprint 03, task S3-19 (`docs/sprints/sprint_03/S3-19_filter_options_endpoint.md`, owner decisions of 2026-11-06 before the task: O-S3-6 and decisions 1–6, all as proposed).

| # | Change | Driver |
|---|---|---|
| C107 | **Catalog filter options as built (§4.3, §7.1, §7.2, §9.6).** (1) **Endpoint style (O-S3-6):** module endpoints are **minimal APIs**, `internal` in the module; the module makes public only `Map<Name>Endpoints(this IEndpointRouteBuilder)` on its registration class, so ARC-03 holds without contract DTOs. The host maps them on its versioned group: `app.NewVersionedApi().MapGroup("/api/v{version:apiVersion}").HasApiVersion(1, 0).MapCatalogEndpoints()`, so a module needs no versioning package. Every `Map…` call comes before the Swagger set-up, which lists the documents through `app.DescribeApiVersions()` (#176). (2) **Versioned routes (#153):** only the new module endpoints live under `/api/v1`; the legacy controllers stay at `/api/<controller>` until their steps retire them, and the dead second route of `BaseCrudController` is gone. The API version is read **from the URL segment only**; the query, header and media-type readers made Swagger list `api-version` and `v` twice on every operation. (3) **`GET /api/v1/catalog/filter-options`** answers in the shape of SPEC §8.1: the four layers with every filter value; use cases sorted by German name (they have no sort order), the other layers by `SortOrder`; inactive use cases are not listed but are accepted in `useCase`, and their rules apply (CAT-18). `excludedBy[]` lists the selected higher-layer values whose rule *of the release* excludes the option (CAT-12, CAT-19); a value counts as selected only if the request names it, also a product part with `IsAlwaysSelected`. `appTypesWithoutCriteria[]` stays with `/criteria` (SPEC §8.2, S3-20). (4) **Release and errors:** `release=<version>` names a stored release, active or not; without it the active release applies. An unknown release, or no active release, answers 404 `not-found`; an unknown key 400 `validation` with one error per key as sent (`useCase`, `appTypeIds[1]`); an empty value counts as not given. (5) **Caching (§9.6):** the content of a release and the filter values are cached in memory without expiry (`CatalogReader`); the HTTP answer carries an `ETag` (hash of the body) and gets 304 for a matching `If-None-Match`; `Cache-Control` is `public, max-age=3600, must-revalidate` for a named release and `public, no-cache` for the active one, so the request after `catalog activate` returns the new release (CAT-09) | O-S3-6; owner, S3-19 decisions 1–6; #153, #176 |

### Changes from v20

Sprint 03, task S3-17 (`docs/sprints/sprint_03/S3-17_catalog_activate.md`, owner decisions 1–4 of 2026-11-06 before the task).

| # | Change | Driver |
|---|---|---|
| C106 | **Catalog activation as built (§5.1 „Activation“, §9.6, §10.2).** (1) `catalog activate <version>` is the second command of the API host's command mode (C105), with the same exit codes. (2) **Complete releases only:** every criterion of the release, procedure criteria included, has a catalog-default priority (rule 4 of the priority resolution), and every seeded `TestType` has its procedure criterion (C30); otherwise the command names every gap and writes nothing. A stored release never changes, so a gap is fixed in the folder and imported as a new version (O-S3-5 Q2 (iii); owner, S3-17 decision 1). (3) **One active release:** the release active so far is deactivated in the same transaction; a partial unique index on `IsActive` (migration `AddSingleActiveReleaseIndex`) refuses a second one. An older release may be activated again (rollback); activating the active release writes nothing; no command deactivates the last active release (decision 4). (4) **Cache (§9.6, D10):** `CatalogReader` caches the content of a release in `IMemoryCache` under its `CatalogReleaseId` without expiry and reads which release is active from the database on every call, because the command runs in another process and cannot clear the API's cache; the next request after an activation returns the new release without a restart. Replaces „invalidated when a release is published“ (decision 2). The catalog endpoints (S3-19) read through it (decision 3) | Owner, S3-17 decisions 1–4; S3-12 P4; S3-15 P2; S3-16 P1 |

### Changes from v19

Sprint 03, task S3-16 (`docs/sprints/sprint_03/S3-16_catalog_import.md`, owner decisions 1–6 of 2026-11-06 before the task).

| # | Change | Driver |
|---|---|---|
| C105 | **Catalog import as built (§5.1 „Import“, §10.2).** (1) `catalog import <version>` is a command mode of the API host, not a project of its own: `Program.cs` hands `catalog …` arguments to `CatalogModule.RunCommandAsync`, with only the Platform and Catalog modules, no web server, no migration and no background job; exit code 0 / 1 (refused, nothing written) / 2 (usage). The public entry is a method of `CatalogModule`, so `Catalog.Contracts` stays empty of import types (S3-11 §2.2). (2) The release folders ship with the API image (`/app/catalog/releases`); the setting `Catalog:ReleasesPath` (default `catalog/releases`, relative to the working directory) points there or to the repository folder. (3) A version is imported once. (4) `Criterion.MarkSupersededBy` sets the successor key of a stored criterion once, when the next release lists it in `supersedes`; each such key must be a criterion of the previous release (the one stored last). (5) A standard reference is unique per standard, clause **and URL**, so a corrected URL is a new row cited only by the new revision; an exclusion rule is unique per source, target and reason (both indexes `NULLS NOT DISTINCT`, migration `AddImportUniqueIndexes`). (6) `CatalogImmutabilityInterceptor` refuses every change and delete of a stored catalog row except `CatalogRelease.IsActive` and a first `SupersededByKey` (CAT-05). Also: the key example in §5.1 follows the key scheme of S3-15 (decision 1a) | Owner, S3-16 decisions 1–6; S3-11 §2.2; S3-12 P2; S3-13 P1; S3-15 P1, P3, P9 |

|---|---|---|

`PriorityLevel` and the flags enum `Rating` (six levels, no `Unknown`) are in `Catalog.Contracts` (S3-11 §2.2). **Seed (O-S3-5 D1):** the migration writes the four filter-value tables with fixed ids and the values of Kriterienkatalog §2; the tables generate no ids, because only migrations write them. **Keys** (owner, 2026-11-06), the contract of release folders and API (SPEC §8.1): use cases `hr`, `reporting`, `trainingManagement`, `travelRequests`, `payroll`, `internalCommunication`, `databaseApplication` (inactive); app types `web`, `softwareOpen`, `softwareClosed`, `mobileOpen`, `mobileClosed`, `document`, `hardware`, `relayEmergencyService`; product parts `base`, `audio`, `video`, `twoWayVoice`, `rtt`, `biometrics`, `authoringTool`; test types `softwareTest`, `appTest`, `pdfTest`, `bikWeb`, `bikApp` (procedure criteria `PROC-<key>`). **`AppType.IsInScope` removed** (owner, 2026-11-06): no document defined it; Hardware and Vermittlungs- oder Notrufdienst stay selectable and are handled by `appTypesWithoutCriteria[]` (§7.2, SPEC §8.2). **`UseCaseTemplate.Description` optional:** no document has a text for it, the seed leaves it empty. **Database rules:** `FilterExclusion` has the CHECK constraints of v7 (one source, one target, target layer lower; CAT-13); `Criterion.TestTypeId` is set exactly for `Kind = TestProcedure` (CHECK); `AllowedRatings` names at least one of the six levels (CHECK); one priority rule per revision, app type and use case, one applicability row per revision, app type and product part (unique, nulls not distinct), one procedure text per revision and test type. **Immutability (CAT-05):** exclusions and procedure mapping are added to a release, applicability, priority rules and procedure texts to a revision, all only before `Publish`; ### Changes from v17

Sprint 03, tasks S3-11 (`docs/sprints/sprint_03/S3-11_catalog_design_note.md`, owner decision O-S3-5 of 2026-11-06) and S3-12 (`docs/sprints/sprint_03/S3-12_catalog_core_entities.md`, owner decisions of 2026-11-06 on its plan).

| # | Change | Driver |
|---|---|---|
| C101 | **Catalog core as built (§4.3, §5.1, ADR-014).** `CatalogRelease`, `Criterion`, `CriterionRevision`, `CatalogReleaseCriterion` and `StandardReference` live in `ProcureAccess.Modules.Catalog`, `internal`, with `CatalogDbContext` (schema `catalog`, history `catalog."__EFMigrationsHistory"`, migration `InitialCatalogSchema`); `CriterionKind` is in `Catalog.Contracts` (S3-11 §2.2). **Published** means stored: the import (S3-16) builds a release in memory and seals it with `Publish`, `PublishedAt` is the import time and `NOT NULL` in the database, so every stored release is published (owner, S3-12 decision 1). After `Publish`, adding a revision, publishing again and changing a revision of the release throw a domain error (`PublishedCatalogChangeException`, CAT-05); a loaded release and its revisions are sealed. Only `IsActive` still changes, through `Activate`/`Deactivate` (`catalog activate`, S3-17). A `StandardReference` cited by a revision of a published release is part of that release; it has no method that changes it, so a corrected clause or URL is a new row (decision 2). A release holds one revision per criterion. `PlainLanguageDe`, `RationaleDe` and `Category` are optional, because release 2026.1 is imported before the plain-language titles exist (O-1, K-1; SPEC R-ADM-01). `Criterion.TestTypeId` follows with `TestType` in S3-13 | S3-12; S3-11 §2.1, §2.2 |
| C102 | **Step 2 reworded (§12) after O-S3-5.** The existing criteria are not copied into the catalog (option C): `FilterTypes`, `CriteriaFilters`, `CriteriaFilterExclusions` and `Criteria` stay in the legacy context, and the legacy endpoints keep reading them until the configurator S10 replaces `/filters` (sprint 04) and steps 5/6 retire products and proposals; criterion proposals are kept as they are. The filter values (`UseCaseTemplate`, `AppType`, `ProductPart`, `TestType`) are seeded in a catalog migration (D1). Release 2026.1 is produced from Kriterienkatalog Teil A/B plus the priority document (SPEC R-ADM-01), so the existing criteria reach it by their EN 301 549 numbers, not as copied rows. `catalog import` accepts a release without priorities, `catalog activate` refuses it (Q2 (iii)). Replaces „Map the repository's `CriteriaFilter` … onto them“, „Migrate existing criteria into release 2026.1“ and „The existing filter UI keeps working against the extended contract“ | O-S3-5 (a)–(e); S3-11 §2.4 C, F1 |
| C103 | **The two seed exclusions are named by their entries (§5.1, §12).** `CriteriaFilterExclusion` has a composite key, so „Id 2 and 3“ named no rows: they are the exclusions 17 → 12 and 17 → 13, the second and third entries of `CriteriaFilterExclusionSeed` (HR-Anwendung → Mobile Anwendung (offen) / (geschlossen)) | S3-11 F7, P7 |

### Changes from v16

Sprint 03, task S3-07 (`docs/sprints/sprint_03/S3-07_automapper_replacement.md`, issues #31, #90); owner decision O-S3-4 of 2026-11-05 on the options of the task's first run.

| # | Change | Driver |
|---|---|---|
| C99 | **ADR-015: AutoMapper replaced by hand-written maps (§6, §14).** AutoMapper 15+ fails D12 (RPL-1.5 or commercial) and 14.0.0 carries the unpatched GHSA-rvv3-g6hj-g44x. The maps are plain C#: extension methods (`ToDto`, `ToEntity`, `ToLookupDto`) and `Expression` projections for EF queries in `MODELS/Entities/Mapping/` (`CatalogMappings`, `AccountMappings`); no mapping package. Modules write their maps the same way. ARC-06 checks that every map sets every target member or names it as ignored (`TESTING_v27.md` V89). Rejected: Mapperly (Apache-2.0, compile-time checks, a new dependency for code that mostly retires in M2–M6), Mapster (MIT, runtime mapping like AutoMapper), Facet (generates DTOs, does not fit), staying on AutoMapper 14, a commercial licence | #31; O-S3-4 (a), (b) |
| C100 | **Generic CRUD controllers are read-only (D11, §2.3).** `BaseCrudController` mapped `POST`/`PUT`/`DELETE` for the legacy catalog and proposal controllers without an authorization requirement; an anonymous `DELETE` removed catalog rows. They were removed (PR #201) instead of being fixed (#90): the catalog changes only through the CLI import (SPEC SD20, R-ADM-01), proposals only through their own endpoints until the workflow retires (M5.6). D11 now reads "generic CRUD controllers only for reading catalog data" | #90; S3-07 F7, F8; O-S3-4 (c), (d) |

### Changes from v15

Sprint 03, task S3-05 (`docs/sprints/sprint_03/S3-05_documentation_session.md`, issue #180): findings of sprint 02 and one owner decision of sprint 03 that had been left for "the next ARCHITECTURE version". Apart from C98, no rule changes; C95 writes down a rule the owner set on 2026-10-30.

| # | Change | Driver |
|---|---|---|
| C95 | **Interim rule for O-22 (§4.3, §16.5).** Until O-22 is decided, a `Contracts` project references no other project, so also no other module's `Contracts` project. ARC-03 fails on such a reference (`TESTING_v16.md` V74). Before, the rule was only in TESTING and CLAUDE §7 | Owner, 2026-10-30 (S2-08 D1); S2-08 P1 |
| C96 | **Platform as built includes Problem Details (§4.3).** Since S2-10 Platform answers every error of the API as RFC 9457 Problem Details: validation errors of controllers and minimal APIs, unhandled exceptions as 500 without the exception text, and empty error responses; `type` is `<base>/problems/<name>` with the 13 names of §7.1 (`ProblemTypes` in `Platform.Contracts`). API-01 checks it; legacy actions with a `[Produces]` attribute still send the media type `application/json` (`TESTING_v18.md` V79) | Sprint 02, S2-10 (decision 3), P3 |
| C97 | **`/api/Health` removed (§4.3, C93).** The legacy endpoint that C93 left with the legacy controllers is gone since S2-19 (#122); readiness is only `/health/ready`, also for the E2E stack | Sprint 02, S2-19 P2 |
| C98 | **Services of the hosting platform in the licence rule (D12).** GitHub Actions runners, CodeQL code scanning and Dependabot are services of the hosting platform, not dependencies: they are neither shipped nor kept in the repository, so D12 does not apply to them. Actions that a workflow references (`uses:`) are development tools and follow D12 | Owner, 2026-11-05 (S3-03 decision 3); S3-03 P3 |
| C99 | **Open footer targets (§16.5).** R-SHL-06 names three footer links whose targets do not exist yet. „Kriterium vorschlagen“ is O-6; „Über ProcureAccess“ (O-23) and „Projekt WERTE.IT“ (O-24) are new open points. Until they are decided the footer links only targets that exist (S2-15) | Sprint 02, S2-15 P2 |

### Changes from v14

| # | Change | Driver |
|---|---|---|
| C92 | **Module DbContexts and migration histories (ADR-014, §10.5).** Every module that owns tables has its own `internal` `DbContext` and its own migrations in its project, with the history table `<schema>."__EFMigrationsHistory"` in a schema of the module (Platform: `platform`). The legacy `ApplicationDBContext` keeps `public."__EFMigrationsHistory"` until its last entity has moved. At startup the host migrates the legacy context first, then every `IModuleDatabaseMigrator` (Platform.Contracts) in registration order. A table moves from the legacy context to a module with two migrations: an empty one in the legacy context that only removes the entity from its model (never `DropTable`), and a first module migration that creates the table only where it is missing (`IF NOT EXISTS`), checked by a test that compares both layouts. Closes S2-06 open point P5 | Owner, 2026-10-30 (O-S2-1, option A of S2-07); S2-06 P3, P5 |
| C93 | **Platform as built (§4.3, §5.7, §9.5).** Since S2-07 the Platform module holds the `TimeProvider` registration, the Serilog set-up with the PostgreSQL sink, the log-retention job, the usage-event writer with `UsageEvent`, and `/health/live` and `/health/ready`, with `PlatformDbContext` for `logging."SeriLogs"` and `analytics."UsageEvents"`. `IUsageEventWriter` and `IModuleDatabaseMigrator` are in `Platform.Contracts`; everything else is `internal`. The legacy logging wrapper `IAppLogging<T>` and `/api/Health` stay with the legacy controllers (`/api/Health` is removed with #122) | Sprint 02, S2-07 |
| C94 | **Module references as built (§4.3).** A module project references Platform only through `Platform.Contracts`; only the host `API` references the `Platform` project (owner, S2-06 D1). A module project never references a legacy project (`API`, `DAL`, `MODELS`, `SERVICES`); the host references the modules, and legacy code waits for its migration step (O-S2-1). The contracts project of *Identity & Access* is `IdentityAccess.Contracts` (the project name). Whether a contracts project may reference another module's contracts project is open (O-22) | Sprint 02, S2-06 D1 and P1 |

### Changes from v13

| # | Change | Driver |
|---|---|---|
| C90 | **Logging as built (§9.5):** the sink is the package `Serilog.Sinks.Postgresql.Alternative` (MIT), not a sink of our own; it writes to `logging."SeriLogs"` in batches with a bounded queue. The **migrations own the table** — the sink never creates it — so every environment starts with the same schema. Retention is an **own job** (`LogRetentionJob`) on `TimeProvider`, not the sink's `retentionTime`, which runs on the system clock and could not be tested with `FakeTimeProvider`. Retention 90 days and run interval 1 day are read from `AppLoggingSettings:Retention`; the 90 days are a 🔧 default since SPEC v2.1 (SD24). The job assumes one API instance, as the startup migrations do | Owner decisions D1, D4 of sprint 01, S1-22 (2026-10-25); S1-22 F7 |
| C91 | **Usage events as built (§5.7):** table `analytics."UsageEvents"` with a surrogate key `Id` (EF needs one), a **random UUID v4** set by the database (`gen_random_uuid()`), so the key does not show the order of events within a day (owner, 2026-10-29, #124; never an identity or a time-ordered UUIDv7); `OccurredOn` is the **UTC** day from `TimeProvider`. `IUsageEventWriter` has one typed method per event of SPEC_v2 §9.1. The writer **awaits** the insert in a DI scope and `DbContext` of its own and logs and swallows every failure, so a failure never fails the caller; there is no queue or background service. SPEC R-MET-01 says "fire-and-forget"; whether the request should also not wait (and not retry) is open in #115. *(v26, C111: fire-and-forget through a bounded queue and a background service; #115 closed)* | Owner decisions D2, D3 of sprint 01, S1-23 (2026-10-25); key: owner, 2026-10-29 (#124, PR #128); S1-23 P1, P3 |

### Changes from v12

| # | Change | Driver |
|---|---|---|
| C89 | **Licence rule for dependencies** (owner, 2026-10-27): D12 now states the rule for all dependencies, not only Excel. Shipped dependencies: OSI-approved, no commercial-use restriction, no strong copyleft or reciprocal licence; weak, file-level copyleft allowed if used unmodified; a dual licence counts by the option the project would use. Development and test tools that are not shipped: OSI-approved, no commercial-use restriction. Before, the rule only said "OSI-approved, no commercial-use restriction", which does not exclude RPL-1.5 (AutoMapper 15+) | S1-03 inventory §4, review of PR #32; CLAUDE §2 (no silent conflict resolution) |

### Changes from v10

| # | Change | Driver |
|---|---|---|
| C71 | *(As built since v52: C138.)* **E-mail delivery.** `IEmailSender` in Platform, implemented with MailKit (MIT) over SMTP with TLS; production provider **Lettermint** (EU-hosted, SMTP relay; credentials only from environment variables); Mailpit (MIT) as SMTP catcher in Development and E2E. Message types: address confirmation, password reset, „already registered“ notice, organisation invitation, product-identity request accepted/rejected. Open and click tracking are switched off in the provider project; recipient addresses are not logged above `Debug`. Sent synchronously with one retry (§2.1, §4.3, §9.1, §9.3, §10.1) | SPEC SD13, §7.6 |
| C72 | **Account security.** E-mail confirmation (Identity `RequireConfirmedEmail` for the gated actions, not for sign-in): saving lists, creating organisations, accepting invitations, creating contributor profiles and all publication paths require a confirmed address (403 Problem Details `email-unconfirmed`). Lockout after 5 failed sign-ins for 15 minutes; password at least 12 characters; confirmation link 24 h, reset link 2 h, invitation 14 days, all single use and stored hashed (§9.1) | SPEC R-AUTH-01–04, R-ORG-02 |
| C73 | **Dark theme in v1.** `UserPreferences.Appearance` (`System` / `Light` / `Dark`); anonymous users follow `prefers-color-scheme`. Pure client concern otherwise (§5.2) | SPEC SD11 |
| C74 | **Catalog releases from data files** (*proposal O-S1*, confirmed in v12, C87): a release is a versioned folder in the repository (`catalog/releases/<version>/`, JSON); CLI commands `catalog import <version>` (validates everything, imports atomically as an immutable release) and `catalog activate <version>`. No catalog-editor UI in v1; `CatalogEditor` stays reserved (§5.1, §12) | SPEC R-ADM-01 |
| C75 | **Product-identity administration** (*proposal O-S2*, confirmed in v12, C87): `PlatformAdmin` endpoints to list, accept and reject `ProductIdentityRequest`s and to merge duplicate `SoftwareProduct`s; minimal admin pages; actions logged with user and time (§7.2) | SPEC R-ADM-02 |
| C76 | **Removing a candidate** (*proposal O-S3*, confirmed in v12, C87): `DELETE /api/v1/candidates/{id}` (Owner, Editor); evidences and assessments are deleted, a published entry stays and its `SourceCandidateProductId` becomes null (Y8) (§7.2) | SPEC R-CAN-09 |
| C77 | **Response shapes fixed** for `GET /catalog/filter-options` and `GET /catalog/criteria` (SPEC §8.1, §8.2): `excludedBy[]` carries `layer`, `id`, `name` and **no reason text** (the client builds it from UX keys); `appTypesWithoutCriteria[]` is a list of `{ id, name }`; `matchedRule.kind` ∈ `appTypeAndUseCase | appType | useCase | default`. O-8 closed (§7.2) | SPEC SD10 |
| C78 | **Problem types** with a configurable base URI (SPEC §8.6) (§7.1) | SPEC §8.6 |
| C79 | **Personal data export** `GET /api/v1/account/export` as JSON (SPEC §8.4) and account deletion rules (sole Owner of an organisation with other members is refused; an organisation whose last member deletes the account is deleted with its lists and projects) (§7.2, §9.3) | SPEC SD14, R-AUTH-09/10 |

### Changes from v9

| # | Change | Driver |
|---|---|---|
| C53 | **Project copy of the criteria list (D1).** Assigning a saved list to a procurement project creates a copy owned by the project (`CriteriaConfiguration.ProcurementProjectId`); the copy is not offered for other projects and cannot be shared by link. After assignment the copy's four selection layers (use case, app types, product parts, test types) are fixed; inclusion, priorities and notes stay editable (C51 now applies to the copy). "An assessment exists" means a `CandidateAssessment` with a value other than `Unknown`; assessment rows are created lazily (no row = `Unknown`). Replacing the list is possible until an assessment exists (§5.3, §5.4, §7.2) | D1; owner 2026-10-13; A-F12 |
| C54 | **Vendor entries without assessments (D2).** A Flow E version without criterion assessments stores no `PublishedAssessment` rows; both directory variants return `fulfillment: null` and the UI shows a note instead of K1. If assessments are given, `productPartIds` is required and the full-scope rule (C43) applies. Flow B always has a scope: publication from a project requires the project's criteria list (§4.4, §4.6, §7.2, §7.3) | D2; B-F13 |
| C55 | **Republication only by the original profile (D3).** Only the contributor profile under which a workspace entry was published may republish it; Editors and Owners of the source organisation may withdraw it and publish a new entry under another profile. Republication keeps the entry's `State`; a withdrawn entry is never republished — the next publication creates a new entry (§4.4, §4.6, §5.5, §7.2) | D3; B-F3 |
| C56 | **Content purge on consent withdrawal (D4).** Withdrawing consent for an experience report withdraws it and purges its personal content: one exempt `ContentPurge` operation sets `NarrativeDe`, `AssistiveTechnologyContext`, `ProductVersionUsed` and the assessment notes (`EvidenceNote`) of all its versions to null and keeps a tombstone (ids, dates, state `Withdrawn`, consent timestamp and text version, moderation record). Timing is set by the legal review; default: immediately. Versions are never updated except by `ContentPurge` (§4.5, §5.5, §9.3, §16.2.8) | D4; B-F1 |
| C57 | **Context of experience reports (D5).** App types are optional and always offered; experience reports carry no use case (`useCaseTemplateIds` removed from Flow D) and never match a set use-case filter; `ContributorDeclared` applies to app types only. Allowed ratings come from the stated app types, all six levels without a statement (§4.5, §5.5, §7.2) | D5; A-F6 |
| C58 | **Re-pinning decided (D6, O-3).** `POST /api/v1/configurations/{id}/copies { release: "active" }` as proposed in §16.3.1; a project that should use a newer release is a new project with a copy (§5.4, §7.2, §16.3.1) | D6 |
| C59 | **Share links (D7, O-5).** Shared criteria lists are readable without an account (`GET /api/v1/shared/{token}` → 200). In v1 only organisation lists can be shared (`ShareLink.ResourceType = Configuration`); there are no project share links. `ShareLink` gains `OrganizationId` (global query filter), stores only a hash of the token and is resolved through `IShareableResourceProvider`, implemented by the owning module (authorisation and redacted read DTO) (§4.3, §5.3, §7.2, §9.1, §16.2.3) | D7; B-F14 |
| C60 | **Session implementation (completes C50, C52).** Sign-in time stored in `AuthenticationProperties.Items`; absolute limit checked in `OnValidatePrincipal`, chained with the security-stamp validator; extend sets `ExpiresUtc` explicitly; `OnCheckSlidingExpiration` renews on every authenticated request except the session probe; expiry times in response headers; anonymous `GET /auth/session` returns 200 and issues the antiforgery token, which rotates on sign-in and sign-out; antiforgery failures return Problem Details; on 401 the shell opens an in-app login overlay and retries the request; Data Protection keys persisted in the database; password change and account deletion end other sessions; lifetimes and warning lead time configurable, time read through `TimeProvider` (§8.3, §9.1, §9.4, §10.4, ADR-006) | B-F7–B-F10, B-F12, B-F17 |
| C61 | **Migration plan:** step 2 adds `CatalogReleaseAppTypeProcedure`; step 3 adds cookie authentication with antiforgery, the Data Protection key store, the Configurator entities and `ShareLink`; step 4 becomes session warning and hardening (§12) | A-F13; B-F11, B-F18 |
| C62 | **Module projects.** One .NET project per module plus one `Contracts` project per module; Platform may be used by every module; the dependency table names `Catalog.Contracts`; outside `Contracts` only controllers and the module registration are public; `PersistedConfigurationSource` moves into Export behind `IConfigurationReader` (Configurator.Contracts), which removes the Export ↔ Configurator cycle (§4.3, §4.4) | A-F19; B-F6 |
| C63 | **Version reference in moderation.** `ContentReport`, `CounterStatement` and `ModerationAction` carry a nullable `PublishedEntryVersionId` (the version that was shown) in addition to the entry (§5.6) | B-F3 |
| C64 | **Idempotency.** `PublishedEntryVersion.ClientRequestId` with a unique index on that column alone, plus `RequestHash`; Flow B carries `clientRequestId`; the same id with a different payload returns 409 (§7.1, §7.2) | A-F8; B-F4 |
| C65 | **Test evidence and product versions.** A new product version updates the existing evidence row of that procedure (`TestedVersion`, date); the history lives in the published versions (§5.4; corrects the wording of C51) | B-F15 |
| C66 | **Enforcement of two invariants.** "`WasTested` is not null for test-result origins" is a factory rule, not a cross-table CHECK; immutability of versions is enforced by an EF Core `SaveChangesInterceptor` (§5.5) | B-F5, B-F16 |
| C67 | **API details:** directory filter `testKinds[]` (multi); `EvidenceUrl` on `PublishedTestEvidence`; derived `procedureAnswers[]` returned by the server; `PATCH …/criteria/{criterionRevisionId}`; Flow D step 2 reads "no `PublicationConsent`" (§4.5, §5.5, §7.2, §7.3) | A-F7, A-F11; B-F19 |
| C68 | **Wording aligned:** consent is no longer described as conditional (§9.3, §16.2.8); §13 uses the WSP-02/03 wording for the project list; the §11.2 projection runs inside Directory on configuration data read through `IConfigurationReader` | A-F9, A-F10; B-F19 |
| C69 | **Usage events for the PRD §10 indicators** listed (§5.7) | Review A (PRD coverage) |
| C70 | **Open points:** O-3 and O-5 closed; O-14–O-16 added from the UX copy (UX-O2, UX-O8, UX-O19); the number O-10 was never assigned; decisions D1–D7 recorded in §16.4.14 | A-F15, A-F18 |

Configurations and the ad-hoc selection carry **several** app types and optional test types (§4.4, §5.1, §5.3). | Design review 2026-10-06, points 1–4 |

---

## 1. Purpose and scope

This document defines the target architecture for ProcureAccess: module boundaries, the domain model, API contracts, cross-cutting concerns, deployment topology, and a migration path from the existing partial implementation.

The architecture must serve two product pillars defined in the PRD:

1. **Kriterien-Konfigurator** — software type + intended use → prioritised, plain-language accessibility criteria → adjust → export. **Usable without an account up to and including the export** (v4).
2. **Workspace with optional publication** — organisation-scoped procurement projects with candidate products, assessed against criteria, optionally published into a community-maintained directory.

It must additionally serve two contribution paths that belong to *neither* pillar — experience reports (§4.5) and vendor/auditor test results (§4.6).

---

## 2. Context and constraints

### 2.1 Fixed technology decisions

| Layer | Technology | Status |
|---|---|---|
| Backend | ASP.NET Core 10 (.NET 10 LTS), C# | locked (raised from 8 in v7, C41) |
| ORM | EF Core 10 with Fluent API (`IEntityTypeConfiguration<T>`, no data annotations) | locked |
| Database | PostgreSQL 17 (Npgsql) | locked |
| Frontend | Angular 22 (zoneless, standalone, signal inputs), TypeScript, NgRx Signals, Angular Material (M3) | locked (version raised from 20 in v6) |
| Packaging | Docker / docker-compose | locked |
| E-mail | SMTP with TLS via MailKit; provider Lettermint (EU); Mailpit locally | locked (new in v11, C71) |
| Distribution | Open source on GitHub | locked (funding requirement) |

### 2.2 Organisational constraints

- **Team size:** effectively one developer, AI-assisted. Every decision must be justifiable against maintenance cost for a single maintainer.
- **Funding horizon:** ProcureAccess is a research deliverable. Ownership after the funding period is an open question (PRD §11). **Handover-ability is therefore a first-class quality attribute**, ranked above performance.
- **Operating model:** one central instance hosted by WERTE.IT. Self-hosting is not a v1 requirement but is not architecturally excluded, since the open-source release invites it.
- **Workspace ownership:** organisation/team scoped. Colleagues in the same procurement department work on the same Beschaffungsvorgang.

### 2.3 State of the existing codebase

The existing implementation covers parts of the criteria filtering pillar.

**Keep and build on:**
- The four-layer pattern (Entity/DTO → Repository → Service → Controller), appropriate for reference data and economical for a solo maintainer. *Since v17 (C99):* entity–DTO maps are hand-written (ADR-015); AutoMapper is gone.
- `BaseEntity` with `Id`, `CreatedAt` and the PostgreSQL `xmin` concurrency token. Optimistic concurrency matters more now that workspaces are team-shared.
- Serilog, `Result<T>`, Docker setup, ngx-translate i18n, Angular Material.
- The NgRx Signals store composed from `signalStoreFeature` slices — a good fit, but the slice cut changes (§8.1).

**Structurally outdated (predates the Workspace pivot):**
- `withProducts()`, `withProposal()`, the `Approver` role and the `objects:approve` claim implement *"central product database + proposal workflow with editorial approval"* — rejected in brainstorm §2.1 and §2.4, listed as a Nicht-Ziel in PRD §6.
- No Workspace, no organisation concept, no Kanal 1/Kanal 2 separation, no separation of Dokumentationsgrad and Erfüllungsgrad.
- Global-only roles. Organisation-scoped workspaces need resource-based authorisation.
- Criteria modelled as mutable rows. Exported criteria can become contractually binding parts of a tender (brainstorm §2.6), which requires reproducibility, which requires versioning.
- JWT in `localStorage` — replaced by Identity cookie authentication (D8, C50).
- Generic `BaseCrudController<TEntity, TDto, TController>` is fine for reading catalog data (read-only since v17, C100), but Workspace and Directory have state transitions and invariants that must not be exposed as generic CRUD.

---

## 3. Requirements summary

### 3.1 Functional drivers (from PRD §9)

| # | Driver | Architectural consequence |
|---|---|---|
| F1 | Filter by app type (incl. closed software), product parts and test procedure (P0 since PRD v3) | Catalog applicability model; four ordered filter layers with exclusion rules |
| F2 | Filter by use case (Verwendungszweck; formerly "Anwendungstyp-Template"), extensible | Data-driven templates, org-owned templates prepared; inactive templates possible |
| F3 | Criteria in plain language, based on BIT inklusiv (Software-, App-, PDF-Test; formerly BITV-Softwaretest) | Criterion text separate from standard references |
| F4 | Recommended priorities/weights per app type + use case | Rule resolution engine with explainable output |
| F5 | Excel export | Pluggable export pipeline, accessible output; **available without an account** for an ad-hoc selection (D15) |
| F6 | Workspace: create and assess candidate products | Org-scoped aggregate, team collaboration |
| F7 | Optional publication, Kanal 1/2 clearly separated | Publication as explicit snapshot, not a visibility flag; **one write path per channel origin** — Kanal 1 only via Flow E, workspace publication only as Kanal 2 (D16) |
| F8 | "Getestet: ja/nein" always visible | Mandatory non-nullable boolean on every published **test-result** entry (Flows B and E), never redactable; experience reports (Flow D) are not tests and carry no test status (C28); tri-state only inside the private workspace (§5.4) |
| F9 | Dokumentationsgrad and Erfüllungsgrad as two separate signals | No combined score in schema or API contract (§4.4); eight counts returned for test-result entries (§16.4.1, §16.4.5); none for experience reports (C33) |
| F10 | Pseudonymous Kanal-2 participation | Contributor identity decoupled from account **and from organisation membership** |
| F11 | Save, share, propose | Share links for criteria lists, readable without an account (C59); criteria suggestions via an external WERTE.IT channel (§16.4.2); product proposals via `ProductIdentityRequest` |
| F12 | Tool is itself accessible | Accessibility as an architectural constraint (§9.4) |

### 3.2 Quality attributes, ranked

1. **Accessibility** — non-negotiable; the tool loses its legitimacy if it fails its own criteria.
2. **Maintainability / handover-ability** — a future maintainer must onboard from the repository alone.
3. **Privacy and legal defensibility** — special-category personal data (§9.3) and takedown risk (PRD §7).
4. **Traceability / reproducibility** — what was exported into a tender must be reconstructible years later.
5. **Performance and scale** — deliberately last. See §11.

### 3.3 Explicit non-requirements

No high availability, no multi-region, no real-time collaboration, no offline mode, no public write API, no microservices, no message broker in v1.

---

## 4. Architecture overview

### 4.1 Style: modular monolith

One deployable ASP.NET Core application with enforced internal module boundaries.

*Rationale:* a single maintainer, low request volume (§11), and a handover requirement all argue against distributed deployment. Module boundaries give most of the comprehension benefits of services without the operational cost. **→ ADR-001**

### 4.2 Component diagram

```
┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  Browser — Angular 22 SPA (zoneless, NgRx Signals)             │
│  lazy-loaded features: catalog · configurator · workspace ·    │
│                        directory · contribute · admin · account│
└────────────────────────┬───────────────────────────────────────┘
                         │ HTTPS · JSON · cookie-based session
┌────────────────────────▼───────────────────────────────────────┐
│  Reverse proxy (nginx or Caddy)                                │
│  TLS · static assets · security headers · gzip/brotli          │
└────────────────────────┬───────────────────────────────────────┘
┌────────────────────────▼───────────────────────────────────────┐
│  ASP.NET Core 10 API — single deployable, modular monolith     │
│                                                                │
│  ┌──────────┐ ┌─────────────┐ ┌───────────┐ ┌──────────────┐   │
│  │ Catalog  │ │ Configurator│ │ Workspace │ │  Directory   │   │
│  └──────────┘ └─────────────┘ └───────────┘ └──────────────┘   │
│  ┌──────────┐ ┌─────────────┐ ┌───────────────────────────┐    │
│  │Moderation│ │   Export    │ │  Identity & Access        │    │
│  └──────────┘ └─────────────┘ └───────────────────────────┘    │
│  ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────    │
│  Platform: AuthZ handlers · caching · ProblemDetails ·         │
│            Serilog · i18n · rate limiting · file storage       │
└──────────┬──────────────────────────────────┬──────────────────┘
           │ EF Core / Npgsql                 │ file I/O
┌──────────▼──────────────────────┐  ┌────────▼───────────────────┐
│  PostgreSQL 17                  │  │  File store (volume, S3-   │
│  schemas: catalog · configurator│  │  compatible later)         │
│  workspace · directory ·        │  │  ACR/PDF uploads, exports  │
│  moderation · identity ·        │  │  served as attachments     │
│  analytics · logging            │  │                            │
└─────────────────────────────────┘  └────────────────────────────┘
```

### 4.3 Module responsibilities and dependency rules

| Module | Owns | May depend on |
|---|---|---|
| **Catalog** | Criteria, revisions, releases, app types, product parts, use-case templates, test types, priority rules, procedure mapping. *As built (v18, C101; v19, C104):* releases, criteria, revisions and standard references with `CatalogDbContext` (ADR-014); the four filter layers (seeded), exclusions, procedure mapping, applicability, priority rules and procedure texts; *v22 (C107):* `GET /api/v1/catalog/filter-options` as a minimal API; resolution follows in S3-20; *v37 (C123):* `ICatalogSelectionResolver` in its contracts, the server-side resolution of a selection for the export; *v69 (C155):* `ICatalogReleaseCarryOver`, a saved selection carried over to the active release | *no module* (leaf) |
| **Configurator** | Saved criteria configurations (organisation lists and project copies, C53), user overrides, copies to a newer release. *As built (v66, C152):* `ConfiguratorDbContext` (schema `configurator`) with the saved lists, `IConfigurationReader` and `ICriteriaListDataExport`. *As built (v67, C153):* save, read and list below `/api/v1/configurations` and `IConfigurationDraftCheck`, the draft check of the export and of saving; *(v68, C154)* the client's S12, S14 and the saved list read only; *(v69, C155)* a decision, the selection, delete and the copy to the active release on the server; *(v70, C156)* their screens | Catalog.Contracts, IdentityAccess.Contracts |
| **Workspace** | Organisations' procurement projects, candidate products, test evidences, private assessments. *As built (v86, C172):* `WorkspaceDbContext` (schema `workspace`) with `ProcurementProject` and the project endpoints (list, create, read, name and status); *(v91, C179)* `CandidateProduct` with test status, test evidences and the private rows beside both, and their endpoints; *(v92, C180)* the project's copy of a list; *(v94, C182)* `CandidateAssessment` with its endpoints, the counts on the project and candidate views and the lock of the list | Catalog.Contracts, Configurator.Contracts, **Directory.Contracts** (publication, product lookup), IdentityAccess.Contracts |
| **Directory** | Software product identities, published entries and versions, ACRs, experience reports, consent records | Catalog.Contracts, **Configurator.Contracts** (configuration-scoped projection via `IConfigurationReader`), IdentityAccess.Contracts |
| **Moderation** | Content reports, counter-statements, moderation audit log | Directory.Contracts, IdentityAccess.Contracts |
| **Export** | Format renderers (XLSX, CSV, later Vergabetext); export sources `PersistedConfigurationSource` and `AdHocSelectionSource`. *As built (v37, C123):* `AdHocSelectionSource` behind `POST /api/v1/exports`, `ExportFileWriter` and one placeholder formatter per format until S4-20; no tables. *As built (v66, C152):* `PersistedConfigurationSource` behind `IConfigurationReader`; *(v69, C155)* behind `POST /api/v1/configurations/{id}/exports`. *As built (v67, C153):* `AdHocSelectionSource` checks the draft through `IConfigurationDraftCheck` | Catalog.Contracts, Configurator.Contracts (`IConfigurationReader`, `IConfigurationDraftCheck`) |
| **Identity & Access** (project `IdentityAccess`) | Users, memberships, contributor profiles, roles, Data Protection key store. *As built (v27, C112):* the Data Protection key store with `IdentityAccessDbContext` (schema `identity`, ADR-014); *v44 (C130):* the contributor-profile tables, no endpoints yet; *v45 (C131):* the key ring encrypted at rest with a certificate; users and roles were legacy until v81, the platform roles are granted with the `roles` command; *v58 (C144):* `Organization` and `OrganizationMembership` (Owner / Editor / Reader), the per-request organisation context and the resource-based `OrganizationRoleRequirement` of its contracts; *v81 (C167):* the users and roles themselves — ASP.NET Core Identity on `IdentityAccessDbContext`, the endpoints of sign-in, registration, password reset, account and organisations, the `roles` command, the seeded users; its contracts declare `ICriteriaListDataExport` and `IOrganizationDataDeletion`, which the modules that own the data implement, and `IUserDirectory`, the display name of a user for another module | *no module* |
| **Platform** | Share links (resolved through `IShareableResourceProvider`, C59), file storage, e-mail sending (`IEmailSender`, C71), ProblemDetails, rate limiting, i18n, caching. *As built (v15, C93):* `TimeProvider`, logging with retention (§9.5), usage events (§5.7), health endpoints, RFC 9457 Problem Details for every error response (§7.1; v16, C96), with `PlatformDbContext` (ADR-014); e-mail sending with MailKit (v52, C138); *v58 (C144):* `IOrganizationContext` in `Platform.Contracts`, the organisation set of the request that every module's query filter reads, implemented by IdentityAccess; *v72 (C158):* share links (`IShareLinks`, `IShareableResourceProvider` in `Platform.Contracts`, the anonymous read `GET /shared/{token}`) | *no module*; **every module may use Platform** through `Platform.Contracts` (not repeated in the rows above) |

**Contracts and projects (v7, C44; enforced by project references since v10, C62).** Each module is one .NET project (`ProcureAccess.Modules.<Name>`) with a separate contracts project (`ProcureAccess.Modules.<Name>.Contracts`, interfaces and DTOs). A module project references only the contracts projects listed in the table and `Platform.Contracts` (v15, C94: never the `Platform` project, which only the host references, and never a legacy project), so the compiler rejects any other dependency, and `internal` has an effect because modules are separate assemblies. **A contracts project references only the contracts projects of the modules its row lists** (v66, C152, ADR-017; owner, 2026-11-16 — O-22 decided; until then it referenced no project, C95): not `Platform.Contracts`, not a module project, and the table lets no contracts project reach itself; ARC-03 fails on any other reference. Inside a module project only the controllers (needed for MVC discovery) and the module's registration extension (`Add<Name>Module`) are public; for Platform the registration class `PlatformModule` also maps the module's endpoints (`MapPlatformEndpoints`). A module never references another module's entities or `DbContext`; a module that owns tables has its own `internal` `DbContext` (ADR-014). Workspace never references Directory entities: Flow B calls `IPublicationGateway` (implemented in Directory, wraps `PublishedEntryFactory`) and passes a redacted `PublicationRequest` DTO. Withdrawal rights for workspace-origin entries are resolved through `IEntrySourceAuthorizer`, declared in Directory.Contracts and implemented by Workspace (dependency inversion, so Directory never reads workspace tables). Configurations are read by other modules only through `IConfigurationReader` (declared in Configurator.Contracts, implemented in Configurator); this is how `PersistedConfigurationSource` in Export and the configuration-scoped directory projection get their data, so Configurator never depends on Export and there is no cycle (C62). Share links are resolved through `IShareableResourceProvider` (declared in Platform, implemented by Configurator in v1, C59). NetArchTest asserts exactly the dependencies in the table above.

Rules:

```
Catalog is a leaf — it depends on no other module.
Modules reference each other only through *.Contracts (table above);
  every module may use Platform.
Workspace and Directory MUST NOT reference each other's entities.
Directory has exactly THREE write paths into PublishedEntry:
  (a) PublicationService       — Workspace → Directory crossing (§4.4, Flow B)
                                 channel is always Independent (Kanal 2)
  (b) ContributionService      — experience report, no Workspace (§4.5, Flow D)
                                 channel is always Independent (Kanal 2)
  (c) VendorPublicationService — vendor/auditor test result, no Workspace
                                 (§4.6, Flow E); channel is always
                                 VendorOrAuditor (Kanal 1)
All three converge on the same invariants (channel, contributor, consent,
release pinning, test status), enforced in a shared PublishedEntryFactory;
none may bypass it. The factory accepts Channel = VendorOrAuditor only
together with OriginKind = VendorPublication.
Product identity creation is shared by (a) and (c) through one
IProductIdentityResolver; (b) may only link to an existing identity.
Moderation only reads and state-transitions Directory entries;
it never mutates their content.
```

**History:** v1 stated that `PublicationService` was the *only* boundary crossing (wrong, corrected in v2 with Flow D). v4 adds Flow E. The invariant that survives every version is not "one service" but "several paths, one set of invariants, enforced in a shared `PublishedEntryFactory`".

Enforced by namespace conventions plus **NetArchTest** assertions in the test suite, so a violation fails CI rather than being caught in review. This matters disproportionately for a solo project.

### 4.4 Core data flows

**Flow A — Criteria configuration and export**

```
User selects, in this order (each layer may exclude values of the lower ones):
  1 UseCaseTemplate? (single; null = "Keiner davon passt")
  2 AppTypes[]        (multi)
  3 ProductParts[]    (multi; Basisfunktionalität always included)
  4 TestTypes[]       (multi, optional)
      │
      ▼
CatalogQueryService                              (public, no account)
      ├─ validate selection against FilterExclusion rules (400 if violated)
      ├─ applicable criteria   = f(AppTypes, ProductParts) ∪ one procedure criterion per TestType (C30)
      ├─ allowed ratings       = ∪ AllowedRatings of the procedures mapped to the AppTypes
      │                          in this release (C31, C45)
      ├─ priority per criterion = resolve(criterion, appTypes, useCase)
      └─ pinned to the active CatalogRelease
      ▼
Draft selection in the browser (withConfigurator, in memory)
user may include/exclude/re-prioritise/annotate
      │
      ├── A1: export without account ──────────────────────────────┐
      │     POST /api/v1/exports  { selection, format }            │
      │     AdHocSelectionSource: re-resolves priorities on the     │
      │     server, applies overrides, persists NOTHING             │
      │                                                             ▼
      │                                           ExportService → IExportFormatter
      │                                                             ▲
      └── A2: save (account + organisation)                        │
            POST /api/v1/configurations  ──► CriteriaConfiguration │
            POST /api/v1/configurations/{id}/exports ──────────────┘
                 PersistedConfigurationSource (Export, reads the
                 configuration through IConfigurationReader, C62)
```

**Anonymous export (new in v4, D15).** The primary persona should get from "what do I need to demand?" to a file in the Vergabeunterlagen without registering (PRD §5, goal 1). Persistence, sharing and team work keep requiring an account and an organisation, because `CriteriaConfiguration` is organisation-owned and that ownership is what makes it shareable and auditable.

Design points:

- **The server never trusts client-resolved priorities.** The ad-hoc request carries the selection (`releaseVersion`, `useCaseTemplateId?`, `appTypeIds`, `productPartIds`, `testTypeIds`) and per-criterion user decisions (`criterionRevisionId`, `isIncluded`, `overrideLevel?`, `overrideWeight?`, `note?`). The server resolves recommended priorities itself, rejects revision ids that are not part of the stated release or not applicable to the selection (Problem Details, 400; procedure criteria count as applicable through `testTypeIds`, Y7), and renders the same columns as a persisted export — recommended level, user override, weight, note.
- **One formatter, two sources.** `IExportSource` has two implementations, both in Export: `PersistedConfigurationSource` (reads the configuration through `IConfigurationReader`, C62) and `AdHocSelectionSource` (depends on Catalog.Contracts and, of Configurator.Contracts, only on `IConfigurationDraftCheck` — never on a saved configuration; v67, C153). Identical input yields identical file content apart from generation metadata. This keeps EXP-01–04 valid for both and avoids a second rendering code path.
- **Nothing is stored.** No configuration row, no file in the file store, no user-derived data in logs. A `UsageEvent` (`ExportCreated`, dimension: format) is the only trace (§5.7).
- **Reproducibility is preserved** because the file states the catalog release version (EXP-03); the release itself is immutable (ADR-002). What is lost for anonymous exports is only the *server-side* record of the user's overrides — acceptable, since the file is the record.
- **Abuse surface:** an unauthenticated POST that does CPU work. Mitigated by a dedicated rate-limit policy, a request-size cap (bounded by the release's criterion count) and length caps on notes (§9.2).
- **As built (v38, C124):** the files are written from one column model (`CriteriaColumns`) with texts from the UX copy embedded in the Export module (`Texts/de.json`, checked by `check-i18n.mjs`); the CSV guards text cells against formulas. The XLSX (v39, C125) holds the data as the Excel table `Kriterien` and a sheet „Info“.
- **As built (v37, C123):** the request names every criterion of the selection exactly once (O-S4-57 (a)); only the active release is exported, a release replaced since the list was loaded is 400 `release-mismatch` (O-S4-60 (a)); `AdHocSelectionSource` reads the catalog through `ICatalogSelectionResolver`, the code path of the criteria endpoint.
- **As built (v67, C153):** the request check is `IConfigurationDraftCheck` of the Configurator module, shared with `POST /api/v1/configurations`, so a draft the export accepts can be saved and the other way round; only an inactive use case differs (accepted by the export, refused for a new list, CAT-18).
- **Draft carry-over:** the draft lives in the NgRx store. Login and registration use the in-app `login` outlet, so the store survives; after login the draft can be saved as a `CriteriaConfiguration` without being re-entered (§8.1). The draft is not written to browser storage.

**Flow B — Publication from a workspace (Kanal 2 only, changed in v4)**

```
CandidateProduct  (private, organisation-scoped)
      │  user action: "veröffentlichen"
      │  { contributorProfileId, productIdentity, clientRequestId }
      ▼
PublicationService
   0. require: the project has its criteria list (its own copy, C53);
      otherwise 422 — Flow B always has a scope (C54)
   1. resolve product identity → IProductIdentityResolver
      (trigram search, user confirms, or create a new SoftwareProduct)
   2. redact: internal notes, tester qualification note, contacts,
      commercial data are NEVER copied
   3. require: contributor profile; channel is fixed: Independent.
      Candidate already has a visible entry: same profile → new version
      of that entry (State kept); other profile → 409, withdraw first
      (C55). A withdrawn entry is never republished; the next
      publication creates a new entry
   4. require: TestStatus ≠ Unknown (mapped to WasTested true/false);
      copy the candidate's TestEvidence rows (redacted)
   5. scope = requirement criteria included in the project's list;
      one assessment row per criterion in scope (Unknown where the
      candidate has no row or Unknown, C43, C53)
      + CatalogReleaseId + timestamp
   6. context: AppTypeIds and UseCaseTemplateId of the project's
      criteria list (ContextSource = ProjectSelection, C32); no use case
      if the list says "Keiner davon passt"
      ▼
IPublicationGateway ─► PublishedEntryFactory ─► PublishedEntry
                                            └─► PublishedEntryVersion (new, immutable)
```

Publication is a **copy, not a flag**. Consequences, all intentional:

- Editing a workspace entry never silently changes public content; republishing is an explicit act.
- Redaction is structural, not a UI concern — private and public representations are different types.
- Withdrawal removes the public entry without touching the user's own procurement record.

*Trade-off:* published data can go stale relative to the workspace. Mitigated by showing the publication date prominently and prompting the owner to republish after significant changes. **→ ADR-004**

**Why Kanal 2 only (D16).** A procuring organisation is never the vendor of the product it evaluates. Its publication is independent by definition — including when it commissioned an accessibility test itself, since the commissioning party is then the buyer, not the vendor. Offering a channel choice in the workspace would invite exactly the mislabelling the two-channel principle exists to prevent, and would scatter consent capture across two UIs. Kanal 1 has a single entry point: Flow E (§4.6). `PublicationConsent` is therefore no longer part of Flow B.

**Flow C — The two signals**

```
GET /api/v1/directory/products/{id}/entries?configurationId={cfg}   (authenticated)
GET /api/v1/directory/products/{id}/entries                          (public)

PublishedEntry ─┐
                ├─► projection computed per request ─► { documentation, fulfillment }
CriteriaConfig ─┘   (second form: projected against the entry's own criterion scope)
```

**Erfüllungsgrad is relative to the requesting user's criteria selection and therefore cannot be a stored property of a product.** Dokumentationsgrad *is* an attribute of the entry (what evidence exists, how recent) and is stored — in a separate DTO branch that the API contract never merges. **→ ADR-003**

A Flow E version published without criterion assessments has no scope. Both variants return `fulfillment: null` for it, and the UI shows a note instead of K1 (C54). Every other test-result entry has a scope and a full breakdown.

**What this does and does not guarantee.** v1 claimed a combined score was "structurally impossible". That overstated the protection. The accurate statement:

| Layer | Guarantee |
|---|---|
| Persistence | **Hard.** No column, view or index holds an aggregate accessibility score. Nothing can be sorted or filtered by one. |
| API contract | **Hard for ratios, soft for counts.** No percentage, rating or `overallStatus` field is ever serialised; a contract test asserts this. Category counts *are* returned, and a client could divide them. |
| Presentation | **Convention only.** Enforced by the UI rule in §8.4, the domain component `pa-fulfillment-breakdown` (UX copy K1) and a review checklist, not by the architecture. |

The counts are returned as a complete seven-way breakdown (`fulfilled`, `slightRestriction`, `restriction`, `barrier`, `blockade`, `notApplicable`, `unknown`) plus `notCovered` (§5.1), and no total (changed in v5 from five-way, C25). Returning all eight together makes a naive "X %" harder to compute correctly than to compute wrongly — a weak deterrent, and named as such rather than dressed up. **Decided in v4** (Option A, §16.4.1): the stricter per-criterion-only contract was rejected because it would make the directory list and comparison views substantially harder to use.

### 4.5 Flow D — Direct contribution without a workspace (new in v2)

The PRD persona *"Mensch mit Behinderung als Beitragende"* has no procurement project, frequently belongs to no organisation in the tool's sense, and must be able to contribute pseudonymously. Routing this person through a workspace would be wrong three times over: it invents a Beschaffungsvorgang that does not exist, it requires an organisation membership that would tie the contribution to an employer, and it therefore undermines exactly the pseudonymity the PRD demands.

```
Authenticated user with a ContributorProfile
      │  action: "Erfahrungsbericht zu einem Produkt beitragen"
      ▼
ContributionService
   1. select an EXISTING SoftwareProduct (search only —
      this path may not create product identities, see below)
   2. channel is fixed: Independent (Kanal 2); no PublicationConsent
      (that record belongs to Kanal 1 only, C67)
   3. contributor profile required; organisation membership is NOT read
   4. pin CatalogReleaseId (for the optional per-criterion assessment)
   5. optional context: AppTypeIds[] stated by the contributor
      (ContextSource = ContributorDeclared); no use case (C57);
      validated against FilterExclusion
   6. consent required for every report (C48); withdrawal of consent
      = withdrawal + ContentPurge (C56)
      ▼
PublishedEntryFactory ─► PublishedEntry { originKind: DirectContribution }
      └─► ExperienceReport (narrative + assistive-technology context)
          no PublishedTestEvidence — an experience report is not a test
```

Design points:

- **This path cannot create a `SoftwareProduct`.** Identity creation stays with Flows B and E, where a procurement context or the vendor's own knowledge gives the name and vendor some grounding. A contributor wanting to report on an unlisted product submits a product-identity request that a `PlatformAdmin` resolves. This keeps §5.5's identity hygiene intact without blocking the persona.
- **`PublishedEntry` carries no `OrganizationId`.** Provenance is expressed by `ContributorProfileId` and `OriginKind` only. An organisation-derived entry is deliberately not attributable to the organisation; that is the same competitor-visibility concern that motivates §5.4.
- **No test status (E13, changed in v5).** An experience report describes one person's use of the product, not an accessibility test. The factory creates no `PublishedTestEvidence` for this origin, the read API returns these entries only in `experienceReports` (§7.3), and the UI shows the contribution type (`contribute.report.typeNote`) instead of K4. A contributor who has actually tested the product publishes via Flow E (vendor/auditor) or Flow B.
- **Per-criterion assessment is optional on this path, and no counts are derived from it (C33).** A report has no defined criterion scope, so a projection would be undefined. Only assessed criteria are stored (the C43 full-scope rule applies to test-result entries); the read API returns them as a plain list (`assessments`, possibly empty); there is no `fulfillment` branch on experience reports.
- **Consent for every report (v7, C48); purge on withdrawal (v10, C56).** Not only the assistive-technology field but the narrative itself can reveal a disability (Art. 9 GDPR, §9.3, §16.2.8). Every experience report therefore requires consent: `ExperienceReport.ConsentedAt` (not null) and `ConsentTextVersion` (which wording was agreed to). Withdrawing consent (Art. 7(3)) is implemented through the existing withdrawal endpoint: the entry becomes `Withdrawn`, and a `ContentPurge` sets `NarrativeDe`, `AssistiveTechnologyContext`, `ProductVersionUsed` and the `EvidenceNote` of the report's assessments to null on all versions. A tombstone remains: entry and version ids, dates, state `Withdrawn`, consent timestamp and text version, and the moderation record. The legal review decides the consent wording, the legal basis and the timing of the purge (default: immediately, in the same transaction as the withdrawal); none of these needs a schema change.
- **Context (v10, C57).** The contributor may state app types (optional, always offered); experience reports carry no use case. App types decide which criteria and which ratings the optional assessment offers (all six levels without a statement, §5.1).
- Rate limiting and the moderation flow (§5.6) apply identically to all paths.

### 4.6 Flow E — Direct Kanal-1 publication by vendors and auditors (new in v4)

The PRD persona *"Anbieter / Prüferin"* publishes a test result with the consent of whoever commissioned the test, as a visibility and reputation channel. Like the Flow D persona, this person procures nothing; v3 forced them through an organisation and an invented procurement project (§16.4.3). Flow E removes that detour.

```
Authenticated user with a ContributorProfile of Kind = Vendor | Auditor
      │  action: "Prüfergebnis veröffentlichen"
      ▼
VendorPublicationService
   1. resolve product identity → IProductIdentityResolver
      (same step as Flow B: trigram search, user confirms, or create)
   2. channel is fixed: VendorOrAuditor (Kanal 1)
   3. require PublicationConsent { ConsentGivenBy, OnBehalfOf,
      ConsentedAt, EvidenceNote? }
   4. require a definite WasTested (bool) and, if true, ≥ 1 test
      evidence: TestKind (Procedure / Other / Own), TestTypeId or
      OtherTestTypeName, optional TestedVersion, tester, date,
      EvidenceUrl (C46, C67)
   5. context: AppTypeIds[] (≥ 1) and UseCaseTemplateIds[] (0..n),
      ContextSource = VendorDeclared — shown as "vom Anbieter angegeben";
      validated against FilterExclusion
   6. optional per-criterion assessments. With assessments:
      ProductPartIds required; scope = requirement criteria applicable
      to the stated AppTypes + ProductParts in the active release; one
      row per criterion in scope (Unknown if empty, C43). Without
      assessments: no rows, fulfillment = null (C54).
      Pin CatalogReleaseId
   7. optional ConformanceReport (VPAT/ACR, P1)
   organisation membership is NOT read
      ▼
PublishedEntryFactory ─► PublishedEntry { originKind: VendorPublication }
```

Design points:

- **Identity creation is allowed on this path.** A vendor naming its own product is the best-grounded identity creation the system will ever see; queueing it behind `ProductIdentityRequest` (§16.2.6) would penalise the persona the PRD treats as an incentive mechanism. Flow B and Flow E share one `IProductIdentityResolver`, so duplicate detection behaves identically. Duplicates are handled by the existing admin merge (`MergedIntoId`).
- **Republishing creates a new version with renewed consent.** A new test result for the same product is a new `PublishedEntryVersion` of the same `PublishedEntry` (C42), never an in-place edit; `PublicationConsent` belongs to the version and is required again for each one. Reports, counter-statements and the entry URL stay attached to the entry. Only the profile that published the entry may republish it, and a withdrawn entry cannot be republished (C55).
- **Profile kind is self-declared.** Nothing in the system verifies that the user behind a vendor profile works for the vendor, or that the stated consent exists. Flow A of v3 (workspace route) would not have verified this either. The resulting risks — impersonation, publication about a competitor's product, a vendor posting "independent" praise under a second, individual profile — are recorded for the bundled legal review in §16.2.7 and mitigated, not solved, by moderation (§5.6) and rate limiting.
- **Consent records are never public.** `PublicationConsent` is not part of any directory read DTO; it exists as an evidence record for disputes and moderation.

---

## 5. Domain model

Presented per schema. `BaseEntity` (`Id`, `CreatedAt`, `xmin`) applies throughout; audit columns `CreatedByUserId` / `ModifiedAt` are added where relevant.

### 5.1 `catalog`

| Entity | Key fields | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| `CatalogRelease` | `Version`, `PublishedAt`, `IsActive`, `ReleaseNotes` | Immutable once published |
| `CatalogReleaseCriterion` | `CatalogReleaseId`, `CriterionRevisionId` | Join table; see below |
| `Criterion` | `Key` (stable: the EN 301 549 number as Kriterienkatalog Teil A/B writes it, e.g. `11.1.3.1c`; a PDF/UA-1 clause as `UA1-7.10`; `PROC-<testTypeKey>` for a procedure criterion; v20, S3-15), `Kind` (`Requirement` / `TestProcedure`), `TestTypeId?` (with `TestProcedure`), `Category?`, `SupersededByKey?` | Stable identity across revisions. `Kind` and `TestTypeId` new in v6 (C30). `Category` (≤ 128 characters): the chapter under which Kriterienkatalog Teil A lists the criterion (e.g. „Wahrnehmbarkeit“); none for Teil B and procedure criteria; set by the import, kept by a stored criterion, not used by any endpoint, screen or export (v28, C114) |
| `CriterionRevision` | `CriterionId`, `RevisionNumber`, `TitleDe`, `PlainLanguageDe`, `ShortDescriptionDe`, `RationaleDe`, `SortOrder` | Content; not owned by a release. `PlainLanguageDe` is the plain-language title, `ShortDescriptionDe` the Kurzbeschreibung (ADR-016); both are the project team's texts and optional until it writes them (K-1) |
| `StandardReference` | `Standard` (EN 301 549 / WCAG / BIT inklusiv / ISO 9241), `Clause`, `Url` | m:n to `CriterionRevision` |
| `CriterionProcedureText` | `CriterionRevisionId`, `TestTypeId`, `OriginalNumber`, `OriginalTitleDe`, `ApplicabilityDe`, `RequirementDe`, `RationaleWhyDe`, `AllowedRatings` (flags), `PositiveExamplesDe`, `NegativeExamplesDe`, `SourceUrl` (anchor) | **New in v5.** Verbatim BIT inklusiv texts per procedure (used with permission); 0..n per revision. Testing procedure, delimitation and references are linked, not copied |
| `AppType` | `Key`, `NameDe`, `HintDe?`, `Functionality` (Open / Closed / NotApplicable), `SortOrder` | UI term „Anwendungstyp“. Eight values: Website/Webanwendung, Software (offen/geschlossen), Mobile Anwendung (offen/geschlossen), Dokument, Hardware, Vermittlungs-/Notrufdienst |
| `ProductPart` | `Key`, `NameDe`, `ExplanationDe`, `IsAlwaysSelected`, `SortOrder` | UI term „Funktionsbereich“. Seven values; only `Basisfunktionalität` has `IsAlwaysSelected` |
| `UseCaseTemplate` | `Key`, `NameDe`, `Description?`, `IsActive` | UI term „Verwendungszweck“. Global only; organisation-owned templates (P2) are added when needed (G1, v7). No row for „Keiner davon passt“ (= no template). Inactive templates are not offered for new selections but stay resolvable for old configurations and entries; seed „Datenbankgestützte Fachanwendung“ inactive (v6) |
